(2’)

is an instance of
\Cref
th:relBuechi-to-relReach_Leftarrow_MR with
\mathcal{M}={\mathcal{M}}_{\circlearrowright}
and
\widehat{\mathcal{M}}=\widehat{{\mathcal{M}}_{\circlearrowright}}
.

The key observation is now that the sets
U_{T_{j}}
are absorbing and hence any value achievable for the weighted sum
\sum_{j\in\mathit{ind}(c)}q_{j}{\text{Pr}}^{\widehat{\mathcal{M}},\widehat{\sigma}}_{f(s_{c})}\left(\operatorname{\hbox to8.7pt{\vbox to8.7pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 0.25833pt\lower-1.76527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@closescope \hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lxSVG@roundjoin\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-linejoin} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 3.57 L 5.66 9.23 L 11.32 3.57 L 5.66 -2.09 Z}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope }
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}U_{T_{j}}\right)
can be achieved by the convex combination of two MD schedulers (see reasoning for
\Cref
thm:cruxApproxEqual).
Thus, any value achievable for the weighted sum of reachability probabilities on the quotient can be achieved by a
memoryless
randomized scheduler on the flip-extension of the quotient, and vice versa, i.e., ‘
\eqref{eq:1}\mathbin{\textit{ iff }}\eqref{eq:1p}
’.
We further have ‘
\eqref{eq:2}\mathbin{\textit{ iff }}\eqref{eq:2p}
’.
Putting everything together yields the desired claim ‘
(
1
)
implies
(
2
)
’.
The full proof can be found in
\Cref
app:relBuechi-to-relReach_Leftarrow_proof.
∎

We stress that the claim from
\Cref
th:relBuechi-to-relReach_Leftarrow_MR is an implication, not an equivalence.
We further observe that the construction from
\Cref
th:relBuechi-to-relReach_Leftarrow_MR introduces memory but not randomization.
Given some
memoryless deterministic
scheduler witnessing the constructed relational reachability property on
\widehat{\mathcal{M}}
, however, we can construct a witness for the original relational Büchi property that is again memoryless deterministic.
This observation will be used later for a statement about the complexity of
RelBuechi
in
\Cref
th:buechi_NP-complete.

Corollary 47
.

Let
c\in\mathscr{C}
and
q_{c}\in\mathbb{Q}
, then

\displaystyle\exists\widehat{\sigma}\in\Sigma^{\widehat{\mathcal{M}}}_{\textit{MD}}.\ \sum_{j\in\mathit{ind}(c)}q_{j}{\text{Pr}}^{\widehat{\mathcal{M}},\widehat{\sigma}}_{f(s_{j})}\left(\operatorname{\hbox to8.7pt{\vbox to8.7pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 0.25833pt\lower-1.76527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@closescope \hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lxSVG@roundjoin\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-linejoin} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 3.57 L 5.66 9.23 L 11.32 3.57 L 5.66 -2.09 Z}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope }
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}U_{T_{j}}\right)=q_{c}

implies

\displaystyle\exists\sigma\in\Sigma^{\mathcal{M}}_{\textit{MD}}.\ \sum_{j\in\mathit{ind}(c)}q_{j}{\text{Pr}}^{\mathcal{M},\sigma}_{s_{j}}\left(\operatorname{\hbox to6.98pt{\vbox to6.98pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 0.25833pt\lower-1.11945pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@closescope \hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lxSVG@roundjoin\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-linejoin} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 -1.19 L 0 7.74 L 8.94 7.74 L 8.94 -1.19 Z}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope }
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}\operatorname{\hbox to8.7pt{\vbox to8.7pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 0.25833pt\lower-1.76527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@closescope \hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lxSVG@roundjoin\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-linejoin} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 3.57 L 5.66 9.23 L 11.32 3.57 L 5.66 -2.09 Z}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope }
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}T_{j}\right)=q_{c}

Proof (Sketch).

Let
\widehat{\sigma}\in\Sigma^{\widehat{\mathcal{M}}}_{\textit{MD}}
, then for each MEC
\widehat{\sigma}
must deterministically decide to either stay in the MEC forever or leave the MEC via a selected action.
We construct
\sigma\in\Sigma^{\mathcal{M}}_{\textit{MD}}
as follows:
Outside MECs,
\sigma
behaves like
\widehat{\sigma}
.
Upon entering an MEC
C=(S^{\prime},A)
, if
\widehat{\sigma}
chooses to go to a sink
\bot^{\mathcal{T}}
, then
\sigma
switches to a memoryless deterministic scheduler going to a sub-EC
C^{\prime}
of
C
with
C^{\prime}\in\textit{EC}_{\mathcal{T}}
, i.e., we ensure to visit exactly the target sets from
\mathcal{T}
infinitely often (such a sub-EC and therefore such a scheduler must exist by construction).
Otherwise, if
\widehat{\sigma}
chooses some outgoing action
\alpha\in\textit{Act}\setminus A
with
\alpha\in\textit{Act}(s^{\prime})
for some
s^{\prime}\in S^{\prime}
, then
\sigma
switches to a memoryless deterministic scheduler deterministically going to
s^{\prime}
and taking
\alpha
.
∎

Overall algorithm.

The decision procedure resulting from Steps 1–3 is stated in
\Cref
alg:buechi.

Input:

MDP
\mathcal{M}=(S,\allowbreak\textit{Act},\allowbreak\mathbf{P})
and a
RelBuechi
property

Input:
\displaystyle\exists\sigma_{1},\ldots,\sigma_{n}\in\Sigma^{\mathcal{M}}.\ \sum_{i=1}^{m}q_{i}{\text{Pr}}^{\sigma_{k_{i}}}_{s_{i}}\left(\operatorname{\hbox to6.98pt{\vbox to6.98pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 0.25833pt\lower-1.11945pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@closescope \hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lxSVG@roundjoin\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-linejoin} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 -1.19 L 0 7.74 L 8.94 7.74 L 8.94 -1.19 Z}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope }
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}\operatorname{\hbox to8.7pt{\vbox to8.7pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 0.25833pt\lower-1.76527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@closescope \hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lxSVG@roundjoin\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-linejoin} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 3.57 L 5.66 9.23 L 11.32 3.57 L 5.66 -2.09 Z}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope }
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}T_{i}\right)\bowtie q

//
See
\Cref
prob:relbuechi

1

Output:

Whether the property is true in
\mathcal{M}

//
Step 1: Collect state-scheduler combinations:

\mathscr{C}\leftarrow\{(s_{i},\sigma_{k_{i}})\mid i=1,\ldots,m\}

2

//
Step 2: Build the MEC quotient and define success sets:

\widehat{\mathcal{M}}\leftarrow
MEC quotient of
\mathcal{M}
w.r.t.
(\mathcal{T}_{c})_{c\in\mathscr{C}}

//
See
\Cref
def:MEC-quotient_set

for

i=1,\ldots,m

do

3

//
Collect the sinks in
\widehat{\mathcal{M}}
that represent visiting a set of target state sets
\mathcal{T}
infinitely often that contains
T_{i}

U_{T_{i}}\leftarrow\{\bot^{\mathcal{T}}\mid\exists c\in\mathscr{C}.\ T_{i}\in\mathcal{T}\subseteq\mathcal{T}_{c}\wedge\textit{MEC}_{\mathcal{T}}\neq\emptyset\}

//
See
\Cref
def:success-set

//
Step 3: Solve
RelReach
query:

return

Solve(
\exists\widehat{\sigma_{1}},\ldots,\widehat{\sigma_{n}}\in\Sigma^{\widehat{\mathcal{M}}}.\ \sum_{i=1}^{m}q_{i}{\text{Pr}}^{\widehat{\mathcal{M}},\widehat{\sigma_{k_{i}}}}_{f(s_{i})}(\operatorname{\hbox to8.7pt{\vbox to8.7pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 0.25833pt\lower-1.76527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@closescope \hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {

\lxSVG@closescope }

)

//
See
\Cref
sec:reach

Algorithm 2

Solving
RelBuechi
by reduction to
RelReach

Theorem 48

(Correctness and time complexity)
.

\Cref

alg:buechi adheres to its input-output specification and can be implemented with a worst-case running time of
\mathcal{O}(m\cdot\textit{poly}(2^{m}\cdot|\mathcal{M}|))
, where
m
is the number of probability operators in the property.

Proof.

Since all sets
U_{T_{j}}
are absorbing, we can solve the
RelReach
query from our reduction in
\Cref
th:relBuechi-to-relReach in time
\mathcal{O}(m\cdot\textit{poly}(|\widehat{\mathcal{M}}|))
by the reasoning for
\Cref
thm:correctness.
The size of
\widehat{\mathcal{M}}
is bounded by
|\mathcal{M}|+2^{m}
.
∎

6.2
Complexity of Relational Büchi

Let us now analyze the computational complexity of the
RelBuechi
problem, both over general and memoryless deterministic schedulers, and additionally identify fragments of the problem that are decidable in polynomial time or where memoryless deterministic schedulers suffice.

6.2.1
General Schedulers

Theorem 49
.

Problem
RelBuechi
is strongly
NP
-complete.

Proof (Sketch).

For membership in
NP
, we show that it suffices to guess a polynomial number of MD schedulers in order to guess a witness for the query.
Given some MDP
\mathcal{M}
and
RelBuechi
property
\varphi
, let
\varphi^{\prime}
be the constructed
RelReach
property.
The key observation is that all target sets
U_{T_{i}}
of
\varphi^{\prime}
on
\widehat{\mathcal{M}}
are absorbing and therefore for each
c\in\mathscr{C}
, the goal unfolding of
\widehat{\mathcal{M}}
w.r.t. the target sets for
c
,
\mathcal{T}_{c}^{\prime}=\{U_{T_{i}}\mid i\in\mathit{ind}(c)\}
, corresponds to
\widehat{\mathcal{M}}
with an additional copy of each sink in some
U_{T_{i}}
for some
i\in\mathit{ind}(c)
.
An MD scheduler for
(\widehat{\mathcal{M}})_{c}
can thus be translated to an MD scheduler for
\widehat{\mathcal{M}}
while preserving the reachability probabilities.
By
\Cref
th:relBuechi-to-relReach_Leftarrow_MD, an MD witness for
\varphi^{\prime}
on
\widehat{\mathcal{M}}
can be translated to an MD witness for
\varphi
on
\mathcal{M}
.
Details can be found in
\Cref
app:buechi_NP-complete.

Strong
NP
-hardness follows by reduction from SAT
[
36
]
, see
\Cref
app:buechi_NP-complete for details.
∎

Note that the reasoning for the membership in
NP
from the proof of
\Cref
th:buechi_NP-complete does not imply that MD schedulers suffice for
RelBuechi
.

Under some mild restrictions on the target sets, the procedure detailed in
\Cref
sec:buechi_algo runs in polynomial time.
The following theorem generalizes
\Cref
th:general_PTIME, see
\Cref
app:buechi_general_PTIME for the proof.

Theorem 50
.

The following special cases of
RelBuechi
are in
PTIME
:

(a)

The number of
different
target sets
|\{T_{1},\ldots,T_{m}\}|
is
at most a constant
.

(b)

The target sets
T_{1},\ldots,T_{m}
are all
absorbing
.

(c)

Probability operators with the same scheduler variable and the same initial state have the same target sets
(formally,
\forall i,i^{\prime}.\ (\sigma_{k_{i}}{=}\sigma_{k_{i^{\prime}}}\wedge s_{i}=s_{i^{\prime}})\Rightarrow T_{i}{=}T_{i^{\prime}}
).

We note that
(c)
includes the case that
n=m
(case
(c)
from
\Cref
th:general_PTIME), where every probability operator is associated with a different scheduler variable.

Remark 51
.

A
RelBuechi
query of the form
\exists\sigma.\ \sum_{i=1}^{m}{\text{Pr}}_{s_{0}}^{\sigma}(\operatorname{\hbox to6.98pt{\vbox to6.98pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 0.25833pt\lower-1.11945pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@closescope \hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lxSVG@roundjoin\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-linejoin} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 -1.19 L 0 7.74 L 8.94 7.74 L 8.94 -1.19 Z}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope }
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}\operatorname{\hbox to8.7pt{\vbox to8.7pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 0.25833pt\lower-1.76527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@closescope \hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lxSVG@roundjoin\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-linejoin} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 3.57 L 5.66 9.23 L 11.32 3.57 L 5.66 -2.09 Z}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope }
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}T_{i})\geq m

is a
multi-dimensional percentile query
with a
\limsup
payoff function in the sense of
[
52
]
.
More precisely, it is equivalent to the query
\exists\sigma.\ \bigwedge_{i=1}^{m}{\text{Pr}}_{s_{0}}^{\sigma}(\limsup w_{i}\geq 1)\geq 1
where
w_{i}
assigns weight 1 to all outgoing actions of
T_{i}
.
Such queries can be solved in
PTIME
by
[
52
, Th. 8]
, this case is not covered by
\Cref
th:buechi_general_PTIME.

However,

\Cref
alg:buechi does not necessarily run in polynomial time for queries of the form
\exists\sigma.\ \sum_{i=1}^{m}{\text{Pr}}_{s_{0}}^{\sigma}(\operatorname{\hbox to6.98pt{\vbox to6.98pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 0.25833pt\lower-1.11945pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@closescope \hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lxSVG@roundjoin\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-linejoin} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 -1.19 L 0 7.74 L 8.94 7.74 L 8.94 -1.19 Z}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope }
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}\operatorname{\hbox to8.7pt{\vbox to8.7pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 0.25833pt\lower-1.76527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@closescope \hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lxSVG@roundjoin\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-linejoin} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 3.57 L 5.66 9.23 L 11.32 3.57 L 5.66 -2.09 Z}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope }
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}T_{i})\geq m
, since the number of sink states and thus the size of the MEC quotient may still be exponential in the number of different target sets.

Observe that the analogous special case of
RelReach
is
PSPACE
-hard, as the formula from the proof of
PSPACE
-hardness for
RelReach
is of this form (cf.
\Cref
th:general_EXPTIME).

RelReach
being ‘harder’ than
RelBuechi
can intuitively be explained as follows: In order to reach
m
targets we need to remember which targets we have already seen, but if we want to visit them all infinitely often this is not necessary.

6.2.2

RelBuechi
{}_{\textsf{MD}}
denote the
RelBuechi
problem over MD schedulers.
We can show strong
NP
-completeness analogously to
\Cref

\Cref

Combining
\Cref
th:MD_PTIME
(b)
and
\Cref
th:relBuechi-to-relReach_Leftarrow_MD yields restricted versions of
RelBuechi
for which the procedure detailed in
\Cref
sec:buechi_algo induces MD schedulers.
Let
RelBuechi
≥,>,／≈
denote the fragment of
RelBuechi
restricted to comparison operators
\{\geq,>,\not\approx_{\epsilon}\mid\epsilon\geq 0\}
.

Theorem 54
.

For the following special cases of
RelBuechi
≥,>,／≈
, the procedure detailed in
\Cref
sec:buechi_algo induces MD schedulers, if it returns true:

(a)

Probability operators with the same scheduler variable have the same initial state
(formally,
\forall i,i^{\prime}.\ \sigma_{k_{i}}=\sigma_{k_{i^{\prime}}}\Rightarrow s_{i}=s_{i^{\prime}}
).

(b)

Probability operators with the same scheduler variable have equally signed coefficients and the same target sets
(formally,
\forall i,i^{\prime}.\ \sigma_{k_{i}}{=}\sigma_{k_{i^{\prime}}}\Rightarrow((q_{i}\geq 0\mathbin{\textit{ iff }}q_{i^{\prime}}\geq 0)\wedge T_{i}{=}T_{i^{\prime}})
).

In case
(b)
, the aforementioned procedure runs in polynomial time.

Proof.

(a)
:
If probability operators with the same scheduler variable have the same initial state, then in the constructed relational reachability property also probability operators with the same scheduler variable have the same initial state and additionally all target sets are absorbing by construction.
By
\Cref
th:MD_PTIME
(b)
, the procedure for solving
RelReach
thus induces MD witness schedulers on the MEC quotient, if they exist.
By
\Cref
th:relBuechi-to-relReach_Leftarrow_MD, we can transform any MD scheduler
\widehat{\sigma}
for
\widehat{\mathcal{M}}
back to an MD scheduler
\sigma
for
\mathcal{M}
.

(b)
:
If probability operators with the same scheduler variable have equally signed coefficients and the same target sets, then each set
\mathcal{T}_{c}
must be a singleton.
Hence also in the constructed relational reachability property, we must have
U_{T_{j}}=U_{T_{j^{\prime}}}
for all
j,j^{\prime}\in\mathit{ind}(c)
and hence probability operators with the same scheduler variable have the same target sets.
By
\Cref
th:MD_PTIME
(c)
, the procedure for solving
RelReach
thus induces MD witness schedulers on the MEC quotient, if they exist, and runs in polynomial time by
\Cref
th:buechi_general_PTIME
(c)
.
MD schedulers on the MEC quotient can be translated back to MD schedulers on the original MDP by
\Cref
th:relBuechi-to-relReach_Leftarrow_MD.
∎

Observe that
(a)
includes the case that
n=m
, and thus subsumes both
\Cref
th:MD_PTIME
(b)
and
(c)
.
However, the procedure does not run in polynomial time in case
(a)
since the size of the MEC quotient may still be exponential in
m
.

Corollary 55
.

For the
RelBuechi
variants from
\Cref
th:buechi_MD, MD schedulers suffice.

Corollary 56
.

If all target sets are absorbing,
RelBuechi
{}_{\textsf{MD}}
is in
PTIME
in the cases listed in
\Cref
th:MD_PTIME.

Proof.

A relational Büchi property with absorbing target sets can be reformulated as a relational reachability property.
∎

Summary of
\Cref
sec:buechi

The key insight is that
RelBuechi
can be solved by reduction to
RelReach
on (a slight variation of) the MEC quotient.
This observation yields an algorithm that runs in time exponential only in the number of target sets, but in polynomial time for a fixed number of target sets, absorbing targets, or if each state-scheduler combination only has a single relevant target set.

RelBuechi
is strongly
NP
-complete over both general and MD schedulers; we identified fragments where our algorithm induces MD witnesses in polynomial time.

7
Multi-Objective Relational Reachability

Multi-objective relational reachability properties generalize traditional multi-objective reachability properties
[
12
,
13
,
22
,
51
]
.
The latter ask for a scheduler satisfying multiple predicates comparing a reachability probability to a bound, such as “
Does there exist a scheduler such that the probability of reaching
T_{1}
,
T_{2}
,
T_{3}
from
s
exceeds
q_{1}
,
q_{2}
,
q_{3}
, respectively?
”
We lift this setting to
relational reachability predicates
that compare weighted sums of probabilities—from potentially different states—to a bound, i.e.,
\sum_{i=1}^{m}q_{i,j}{\text{Pr}}^{\sigma_{k_{i,j}}}_{s_{i,j}}(\operatorname{\hbox to8.7pt{\vbox to8.7pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 0.25833pt\lower-1.76527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@closescope \hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lxSVG@roundjoin\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-linejoin} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 3.57 L 5.66 9.23 L 11.32 3.57 L 5.66 -2.09 Z}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope }
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}T_{i,j})\bowtie_{j}q_{j}
.
In the following, ‘predicate’ always refers to a relational reachability predicate unless specified otherwise.
We focus on the multi-objective part here and simplify matters by not considering Büchi objectives.
We conjecture that the approach can be lifted to multi-objective relational properties including Büchi constraints by combining it with techniques from
\Cref
sec:buechi.
Recall that the motivating example from
\Cref
ex:conj_motivating-ex is (the negation of) a multi-objective relational reachability property.

{framedproblem}

[
MORelReach
]

Given an MDP
\mathcal{M}=(S,\allowbreak\textit{Act},\allowbreak\mathbf{P})
, decide whether

\exists\sigma_{1},\ldots,\sigma_{n}\in\Sigma^{\mathcal{M}}.\ \bigwedge_{j=1}^{l}\sum_{i=1}^{m}q_{i,j}{\text{Pr}}^{\sigma_{k_{i,j}}}_{s_{i,j}}(\operatorname{\hbox to8.7pt{\vbox to8.7pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 0.25833pt\lower-1.76527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@closescope \hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lxSVG@roundjoin\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-linejoin} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 3.57 L 5.66 9.23 L 11.32 3.57 L 5.66 -2.09 Z}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope }
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}T_{i,j})\bowtie_{j}q_{j}~,\qquad\text{where}

•

l,m,n
are natural numbers,

•

q_{1,1},\ldots,q_{m,l}
are rational coefficients,

•

q_{1},\ldots,q_{l}
are rational bounds,

•

s_{1,1},\ldots,s_{m,l}
are (not necessarily distinct) initial states,

•

\{k_{1,1},k_{1,2}\ldots,k_{m-1,l},k_{m,l}\}=\{1,\ldots,n\}
is a set of indices,

•

T_{1,1},\ldots,T_{m,l}
are (not necessarily distinct) target sets, and

•

\bowtie_{1},\ldots,\bowtie_{l}\in\{>,\geq,\approx_{\epsilon},\not\approx_{\epsilon}\mid\epsilon\in\mathbb{Q}_{\geq 0}\}
are comparison operators.

Note that, by allowing the coefficients
q_{i,j}
to be zero, we implicitly allow a different number of summands for each predicate.

\lxSVG@closescope }
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}T_{4})\geq 0
.

Intuitively, a
MORelReach
property with the comparison operator
\not\approx_{\epsilon}
contains a disjunction inside a conjunction, since
x\not\approx_{\epsilon}y
is equivalent to
x<y-\epsilon\lor x>y+\epsilon
.
This nested disjunction makes
MORelReach
with
\not\approx_{\epsilon}
computationally more complex than
MORelReach
without
\not\approx_{\epsilon}
.
In fact, we will see that
MORelReach
can be solved in
PTIME
for a fixed number of target sets
only
if we exclude
\not\approx_{\epsilon}
for
\epsilon>0
.
However, the special case of
\neq
(i.e.,
\not\approx_{0}
) can still be handled efficiently.

Remark 58

(Extended MOA Queries)
.

In this section, we work with
multi-objective achievability
(
MOA
) queries in the sense of
[
50
,
24
]
, i.e., queries of the form

\exists\sigma\in\Sigma^{\mathcal{M}}.\ \bigwedge_{j=1}^{l}\mathbb{E}_{s}^{\mathcal{M},\sigma}(\textit{R}^{j})\bowtie_{j}q_{j}

for
an MDP
\mathcal{M}
with state
s
and reward structures
\textit{R}^{1},\ldots,\textit{R}^{l}
,
comparison operators
\bowtie_{1},\ldots,\bowtie_{l}\in\{>,\geq,\approx_{\epsilon},\not\approx_{\epsilon}\mid\epsilon\in\mathbb{Q}_{\geq 0}\}
,
and rational bounds
q_{1},\ldots,q_{l}
.
See
\Cref
sec:related_moa for details.
Note that
[
50
,
24
]
do not allow
\not\approx_{\epsilon}

and that other works such as
[
51
,
22
]
use a more restricted notion of achievability where only
\geq
is allowed as a comparison operator.
In the following, we explicitly call MOA queries with arbitrary comparison operators “
extended MOA queries
” to distinguish in particular from the restricted version with only
\geq
.

Outline of this section.

We first show that multi-objective relational queries can be reduced to extended MOA queries in
\Cref
sec:conj_algo, and outline how to solve the constructed MOA queries in
\Cref
sec:moa.
In
\Cref
sec:conj_complexity, we prove that
MORelReach
is
PSPACE
-hard and decidable in
EXPTIME
.
If all probability operators share the same, absorbing target set, it is
NP
-complete, and if we additionally exclude
\not\approx_{\epsilon}
for
\epsilon>0
, it is in
PTIME
.

7.1
Verifying Multi-Objective Relational Reachability Properties

Throughout this section, we fix an MDP
\mathcal{M}=(S,\allowbreak\textit{Act},\allowbreak\mathbf{P})
and a
MORelReach
property

\displaystyle\exists\sigma_{1}\ldots\sigma_{n}\in\Sigma^{\mathcal{M}}.\ \bigwedge_{j=1}^{l}\sum_{i=1}^{m}q_{i,j}{\text{Pr}}^{\sigma_{k_{i,j}}}_{s_{i,j}}(\operatorname{\hbox to8.7pt{\vbox to8.7pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 0.25833pt\lower-1.76527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@closescope \hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lxSVG@roundjoin\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-linejoin} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 3.57 L 5.66 9.23 L 11.32 3.57 L 5.66 -2.09 Z}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope }
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}T_{i,j})\bowtie_{j}q_{j}

(7.1)

as in
\Cref
prob:morelreach.
In the following, we assume
q_{i,j}\neq 0
for all
(i,j)\in\{1,\ldots,m\}\times\{1,\ldots l\}
for simplicity.
In this section, we outline a procedure for verifying whether (
7.1
) holds, as summarized in
\Cref
alg:conj.
In
\Cref
sec:conj_to_MOMC, we first show how to reduce (
7.1
) to a multi-objective achievability query; and then address how these can be solved in
\Cref
sec:moa.

7.1.1
Reducing to Extended Multi-Objective Achievability

Step 0: Split query (optional).

We define the
class
of a predicate index
j\in\{1,\ldots,l\}
as the set of all objectives
j^{\prime}\in\{1,\ldots,l\}
sharing a scheduler with objective
j
, thus partitioning the set of objectives
\{1,\ldots,l\}
.
Splitting the query is not strictly necessary for the correctness of the approach but improves its practical efficiency (though it does not improve its
theoretical
worst-case complexity).

Definition 60
.

For
j\in\{1,\ldots,l\}
, we define the
class
of
j
as

\llbracket j\rrbracket=\{j^{\prime}\in\{1,\ldots,l\}\mid\exists i,i^{\prime}.\ \sigma_{k_{i,j}}=\sigma_{k_{i^{\prime},j^{\prime}}}\}~.

We let
\textit{ConjPart}=\{\llbracket 1\rrbracket,\ldots,\llbracket l\rrbracket\}
.

For each class of predicate indices we collect the set of all relevant scheduler indices.

Definition 61
.

For
X\in\textit{ConjPart}
, we let

\textit{Scheds}(X)=\{k_{i,j^{\prime}}\mid i\in\{1,\ldots,m\}\wedge j^{\prime}\in X\}
.
For
n_{X}=|\textit{Scheds}(X)|
, we let
\{h^{X}_{1},\ldots,h^{X}_{n_{X}}\}=\textit{Scheds}(X)
.

.

\Cref
ex:conj_running

We have
\textit{ConjPart}=\{\llbracket 1\rrbracket=\llbracket 2\rrbracket,\llbracket 3\rrbracket\}
, i.e., the first and second predicate share a scheduler but not with the third predicate.
More precisely, the first two predicates use
\sigma_{1}
, so
\textit{Scheds}(\llbracket 1\rrbracket)=\{1\}
,
while the third predicate only refers to
\sigma_{2}
, so
\textit{Scheds}(\llbracket 3\rrbracket)=\{2\}
,

We can now split the problem into a number of independent smaller problems, one for every partition element
X\in\textit{ConjPart}
.

.

ConjPart
as defined in
\Cref
def:conjpart, then

\displaystyle\left(\exists\sigma_{2}.\ {\text{Pr}}^{\sigma_{2}}_{s_{1}}(\operatorname{\hbox to8.7pt{\vbox to8.7pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 0.25833pt\lower-1.76527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@closescope \hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {

Note that the first conjunct corresponds to our running example from
\Cref
ex:conj_running.

We can now process each
X\in\textit{ConjPart}
individually.
For the sake of simplicity, we detail the following steps assuming this optional partitioning step was skipped.

Step 1: Collect state-scheduler combinations.

We extend the notion of state-scheduler combinations to multiple objectives, overwriting the definition from
\Cref
def:comb.

Definition 65
.

We define
\mathscr{C}=\{(s_{i,j},\sigma_{k_{i,j}})\mid i=1,\ldots,m,j=1,\ldots,l\}
.
For
c\in\mathscr{C}
, we let
\mathit{ind}(c)=\{(i,j)\mid(s_{i,j},\sigma_{k_{i,j}})=c\}
and
\mathcal{T}_{c}=\{T_{i,j}\mid(i,j)\in\mathit{ind}(c)\}
.
For
j\in X
, we further let
\mathscr{C}^{j}=\{(s_{i,j},\sigma_{k_{i,j}})\mid i=1,\ldots,m\}
and
\mathit{ind}^{j}(c)=\{i\mid(s_{i,j},\sigma_{k_{i,j}})=c\}
for
c\in\mathscr{C}^{j}
.

The number of state-scheduler combinations,
|\mathscr{C}|
, is bounded by
l\cdot m
.

Example 66
.

For the running example from in
\Cref
ex:conj_running, we have

\mathscr{C}=\{(s_{1},\sigma),(s_{2},\sigma)\}
.
Let
c_{1}=(s_{1},\sigma)
and
c_{2}=(s_{2},\sigma)
, then we have

\mathcal{T}_{c_{1}}=\{T,T^{\prime}\}
and
\mathcal{T}_{c_{2}}=\{T\}
, and further

\mathscr{C}^{1}=\{c_{1},c_{2}\}

and

\mathscr{C}^{2}=\{c_{1}\}

Remark 67
.

If we split the query as detailed in Step 0 (p.
7.1.1
), then, by definition, different partition elements do not share schedulers.
Formally, for
X,X^{\prime}\in\textit{ConjPart}
with
X\neq X^{\prime}
:

\{(s_{i,j},\sigma_{k_{i,j}})\mid i=1,\ldots,m,j\in X\}\cap\{(s_{i,j},\sigma_{k_{i,j}})\mid i=1,\ldots,m,j\in X^{\prime}\}=\emptyset~.

Step 2: Unfold targets and set up reward structures.

First, we construct the goal unfolding
\mathcal{M}_{c}=(S_{c},\allowbreak\textit{Act}_{c},\allowbreak\mathbf{P}_{c})
w.r.t. all relevant target sets for each state-scheduler combination
c\in\mathscr{C}
.
As in
\Cref
sec:reach_algo, we use
s_{c}\in S_{c}
to denote the state
(s,\emptyset)
for
c=(s,\cdot)\in\mathscr{C}
.
We then combine all unfolded MDPs
\mathcal{M}_{c}
for
c\in\mathscr{C}
into a single MDP
\mathcal{M}_{\mathscr{C}}
, which corresponds to the disjoint union of the unfolded MDPs plus a fresh initial state
s_{\mathscr{C}}
from which we transition to each unfolded MDP with equal probability.
This enables us to rephrase (
7.1
) as an extended multi-objective achievability query (
\Cref
rem:extended-MOA) on a single MDP.

Definition 68

(Combined MDP)
.

For
c\in\mathscr{C}
, let
\mathcal{M}_{c}=(S_{c},\textit{Act},\mathbf{P}_{c})
be the goal unfolding of
\mathcal{M}
w.r.t.
\mathcal{T}_{c}=\{T_{i,j}\mid(i,j)\in\mathit{ind}(c)\}
as in
\Cref
def:goalUnfolding.
The combined MDP is then defined as
\mathcal{M}_{\mathscr{C}}=(S_{\mathscr{C}},\allowbreak\textit{Act}_{\mathscr{C}},\allowbreak\mathbf{P}_{\mathscr{C}})

where

•

S_{\mathscr{C}}=\{s_{\mathscr{C}}\}\cup\bigcup_{c\in\mathscr{C}}S_{c}\times\{c\}
for a fresh state
s_{\mathscr{C}}
,

•

\textit{Act}_{\mathscr{C}}=\textit{Act}\cup\{\epsilon\}
for a fresh action
\epsilon
,

•

We define
\mathbf{P}_{\mathscr{C}}\colon S_{\mathscr{C}}\times\textit{Act}_{\mathscr{C}}\times S_{\mathscr{C}}\to[0,1]
as follows, distinguishing between the fresh initial state and states from the unfolded MDPs:

–

\mathbf{P}_{\mathscr{C}}(s_{\mathscr{C}},\epsilon,(s_{c},c))=\frac{1}{|\mathscr{C}|}
for
c\in\mathcal{M}_{\mathscr{C}}
,

–

\mathbf{P}_{\mathscr{C}}((s,c),\alpha,(s^{\prime},c))=\mathbf{P}_{c}(s,\alpha,s^{\prime})
for
\alpha\in\textit{Act}
and
s,s^{\prime}\in S_{c}
for some
c\in\mathcal{M}_{\mathscr{C}}
, and

–

all other transition probabilities are 0.

Example 69
.

The combined MDP
\mathcal{M}_{\mathscr{C}}
for the running example from
\Cref
ex:conj_running is depicted in
\Cref
fig:conj_running.

The size of
S_{\mathscr{C}}
is (coarsely) bounded by
1+|\mathscr{C}|\cdot\max_{c\in\mathscr{C}}|\mathcal{M}_{c}|\leq 1+l\cdot m\cdot|S|\cdot 2^{l\cdot m}
.
More precisely, it is exponential in the maximal number of different target sets relevant for some state-scheduler combination.

For each
c\in\mathscr{C}
, we define reward structures
\textit{R}_{T}\colon S_{c}\to\mathbb{Q}
for
T\in\mathcal{T}_{c}
on
\mathcal{M}_{c}
encoding the reachability probabilities as for
RelReach
(cf.
\Cref
def:rewForComb).
Recall that for
RelReach
we defined reward structures
\textit{R}_{c}
for each
c\in\mathscr{C}
that aggregate all reward structures
\textit{R}_{T}
for
T\in\mathcal{T}_{c}
(
\Cref
def:rewForComb).
We now define analogous reward structures
\textit{R}_{c}^{j}
for each predicate
j
and combine them into a reward structure
\textit{R}^{j}
expressing the weighted sum of reachability probabilities for predicate
j
.
We have to scale the collected reward by
|\mathscr{C}|
in order to compensate for reaching each
\mathcal{M}_{c}
with probability
\nicefrac{{1}}{{|\mathscr{C}|}}
from
s_{\mathscr{C}}
.

Definition 70

(Reward structure for predicate)
.

For
c\in\mathcal{M}_{\mathscr{C}}
and
T\in\mathcal{T}_{c}
, let
\textit{R}_{T}
be the reward structure on
\mathcal{M}_{c}
from
\Cref
def:rewForComb.
For
j\in\{1,\ldots,l\}
and
c\in\mathscr{C}^{j}
, we define the reward structure
\textit{R}_{c}^{j}\colon S_{c}\to\mathbb{Q}
on
\mathcal{M}_{c}
with

\textit{R}_{c}^{j}(s,\mathcal{T})=\sum_{T\in\mathcal{T}_{c}\cap\mathcal{T}^{j}}\left(\sum_{i\in\{\mathit{ind}^{j}(c)\mid T=T_{i,j}\}}q_{i,j}\right)\cdot\textit{R}_{T}(s,\mathcal{T})

for
(s,\mathcal{T})\in S_{c}
.
Then, we define the reward structure
\textit{R}^{j}\colon S_{\mathscr{C}}\to\mathbb{Q}
on
\mathcal{M}_{\mathscr{C}}
by letting
\textit{R}^{j}(s_{\mathscr{C}})=0
and

\textit{R}^{j}((s,\mathcal{T}),c)=|\mathscr{C}|\cdot\textit{R}_{c}^{j}(s,\mathcal{T})~.

Example 71
.

The rewards for
\textit{R}^{1}
,
\textit{R}^{2}
are depicted next to the states in
\Cref
fig:conj_running.

Step 3: Reduce to extended MOA query of expected rewards.

We can reduce the quantification over schedulers for
\mathcal{M}
to quantifying over a single scheduler for
\mathcal{M}_{\mathscr{C}}
(
\Cref
def:MDP_combined), and reduce the computation of a weighted sum of probabilities to computing some expected reward, see
\Cref
app:reduce-to-moa_proof for the proof.

Lemma 72
.

Let
\mathcal{M}_{\mathscr{C}}
as in
\Cref
def:MDP_combined and
\textit{R}^{j}
as in
\Cref
def:rewForConj, then

\displaystyle\exists\sigma_{1}\ldots\sigma_{n}\in\Sigma^{\mathcal{M}}.\ \bigwedge_{j=1}^{l}\sum_{i=1}^{m}q_{i,j}{\text{Pr}}^{\mathcal{M},\sigma_{k_{i,j}}}_{s_{i,j}}(\operatorname{\hbox to8.7pt{\vbox to8.7pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 0.25833pt\lower-1.76527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@closescope \hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lxSVG@roundjoin\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-linejoin} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 3.57 L 5.66 9.23 L 11.32 3.57 L 5.66 -2.09 Z}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope }
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}T_{i,j})\bowtie_{j}q_{j}

\displaystyle\mathbin{\textit{ iff }}\quad

\displaystyle\exists\sigma\in\Sigma^{\mathcal{M}_{\mathscr{C}}}.\ \bigwedge_{j=1}^{l}\mathbb{E}_{s_{\mathscr{C}}}^{\mathcal{M}_{\mathscr{C}},\sigma}(\textit{R}^{j})\bowtie_{j}q_{j}~.

We observe that

\displaystyle\exists\sigma\in\Sigma^{\mathcal{M}_{\mathscr{C}}}.\ \bigwedge_{j=1}^{l}\mathbb{E}_{s_{\mathscr{C}}}^{\mathcal{M}_{\mathscr{C}},\sigma}(\textit{R}^{j})\bowtie_{j}q_{j}~

(7.2)

from
\Cref
le:reduce-to-moa
is an (extended) multi-objective achievability query in the sense of
[
50
,
24
]
(see
\Cref
rem:extended-MOA for a details on extended MOA queries).
We will address solving (
7.2
) in
\Cref
sec:moa; let us assume for now that we have a black-box solving such queries.
Note, however, that for solving this multi-objective query it does not suffice to only compute the
optimal
expected reward w.r.t. some reward structures, in contrast to
RelReach
.

Example 73
.

Our running example from
\Cref
ex:conj_running is equivalent to the following property on the combined MDP
\mathcal{M}_{\mathscr{C}}
:

\exists\sigma\in\Sigma^{\mathcal{M}_{\mathscr{C}}}.\ \mathbb{E}_{s_{\llbracket 1\rrbracket}}^{\mathcal{M}_{\mathscr{C}},\sigma}(\textit{R}^{1})<0\land\mathbb{E}_{s_{\llbracket 1\rrbracket}}^{\mathcal{M}_{\mathscr{C}},\sigma}(\textit{R}^{2})>0~.

Consider the memoryless scheduler
\sigma
for
\mathcal{M}_{\mathscr{C}}
defined as follows, which is indicated in
\Cref
fig:conj_running in
bold
:

\displaystyle\sigma(s^{\prime},\emptyset,c_{1})=\alpha

\displaystyle\sigma(s^{\prime},\{T^{\prime}\},c_{1})=\alpha

\displaystyle\sigma(s^{\prime},\emptyset,c_{2})=\alpha

\displaystyle\sigma(t^{\prime},\emptyset,c_{1})=\gamma

\displaystyle\sigma(t^{\prime},\{T^{\prime}\},c_{1})=\delta

\displaystyle\sigma(t^{\prime},\emptyset,c_{2})=\gamma

which induces a
memoryful
scheduler on
\mathcal{M}
.
This scheduler is a witness since

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{s_{\llbracket 1\rrbracket}}^{\mathcal{M}_{\mathscr{C}},\sigma}(\textit{R}^{1})

\displaystyle=2\cdot\left(\frac{1}{2}\cdot\mathbb{E}_{s_{1},\emptyset,c_{1}}^{\mathcal{M}_{\mathscr{C}},\sigma}(\textit{R}^{1})+\frac{1}{2}\cdot\mathbb{E}_{s_{2},\emptyset,c_{2}}^{\mathcal{M}_{\mathscr{C}},\sigma}(\textit{R}^{1})\right)

\displaystyle=\left(\frac{1}{3}\cdot 2+\frac{1}{3}\cdot 2+\frac{1}{3}\left(\frac{2}{3}\cdot 2+\frac{1}{3}\left(\frac{2}{3}\cdot 2+\frac{1}{3}\cdot 0\right)\right)\right)-2=\frac{4}{3}+\frac{16}{3\cdot 9}-2<0~,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{s_{\llbracket 1\rrbracket}}^{\mathcal{M}_{\mathscr{C}},\sigma}(\textit{R}^{2})

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}_{s_{1},\emptyset,c_{1}}^{\mathcal{M}_{\mathscr{C}},\sigma}(\textit{R}^{2})=\frac{1}{3}\cdot 2+\frac{1}{3}\cdot 2+\frac{1}{3}\left(\frac{2}{3}\cdot 2+\frac{1}{3}\left(-2+\frac{2}{3}\cdot 2\right)\right)

\displaystyle=\frac{4}{3}+\frac{1}{3}\left(\frac{4}{3}+\frac{1}{3}\left(\frac{-2}{3}\right)\right)=\frac{4}{3}+\frac{1}{3}\left(\frac{12}{9}+\frac{-2}{9}\right)>0~.

Step 4: Combine partition elements (optional).

If we initially split up the query into the different partition elements
X\in\textit{ConjPart}
in Step 0 (p.
7.1.1
), we need to combine the results for each
X\in\textit{ConjPart}
again to answer the original query (
7.1
) in the end.

Corollary 74
.

Let
ConjPart
as defined in
\Cref
def:conjpart,
\mathcal{M}_{\mathscr{C}}
as defined in
\Cref
def:MDP_combined and
\textit{R}^{j}
as defined in
\Cref
def:rewForConj, then

\displaystyle\exists\sigma_{1}\ldots\sigma_{n}.\ \bigwedge_{j=1}^{l}\sum_{i=1}^{m}q_{i,j}{\text{Pr}}^{\sigma_{k_{i,j}}}_{s_{i,j}}(\operatorname{\hbox to8.7pt{\vbox to8.7pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 0.25833pt\lower-1.76527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@closescope \hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lxSVG@roundjoin\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-linejoin} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 3.57 L 5.66 9.23 L 11.32 3.57 L 5.66 -2.09 Z}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope }
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}T_{i,j})\bowtie_{j}q_{j}

\displaystyle\mathbin{\textit{ iff }}\quad

\displaystyle\bigwedge_{X\in\textit{ConjPart}}\left(\exists\sigma_{X}\in\Sigma^{\mathcal{M}_{\mathscr{C}}}.\ \bigwedge_{j\in X}\mathbb{E}_{s_{\mathscr{C}}}^{\mathcal{M}_{\mathscr{C}},\sigma}(\textit{R}^{j})\bowtie_{j}q_{j}\right)

Proof.

Straightforward from
\Cref
le:split-conjunction and
\Cref
le:reduce-to-moa.
∎

Overall algorithm.

The algorithm resulting from Steps 1–3 is summarized in
\Cref
alg:conj, and the practically more efficient version including Steps 0 and 4 in
\Cref
alg:conj_practice.

Theorem 75

(Correctness and time complexity)
.

\Cref

alg:conj,alg:conj_practice adhere to their input-output specification and can be implemented with a worst-case running time of
\mathcal{O}(2^{l_{\not\approx}}\cdot\textit{poly}(l\cdot m\cdot|\mathcal{M}|\cdot 2^{l\cdot m}))

where
l
is the number of predicates,
l_{\not\approx}
the number of predicates with
\not\approx_{\epsilon}
with
\epsilon>0
, and
m
the number of probability operators per objective in the property.

Proof.

Correctness follows from
\Cref
le:conj_combine, runtime complexity from
\Cref
th:MOA_correctness.
∎

Solving

7.1.2
Solving Extended Multi-Objective Achievability Queries

In this section, we address solving the extended MOA query (
7.2
) constructed in
\Cref
sec:conj_to_MOMC, Step 3 (p.
7.1.1
), i.e.,

\exists\sigma\in\Sigma^{\mathcal{M}_{\mathscr{C}}}.\ \bigwedge_{j=1}^{l}\mathbb{E}_{s_{\mathscr{C}}}^{\mathcal{M}_{\mathscr{C}},\sigma}(\textit{R}^{j})\bowtie_{j}q_{j}~.

To the best of our knowledge, while does exist work on both MOA queries with
positive and negative
rewards
[
51
]
on the one hand, and extended MOA queries with
strict and non-strict
comparison operators
[
22
,
24
]
, on the other hand, these have not been considered together before and, more interestingly, the comparison operator
\not\approx_{\epsilon}
(or even just
\neq
) has not been addressed before.
We refer to
\Cref
sec:related_moa for a detailed discussion of related work on multi-objective model checking.
We extend
[
22
,
24
]
to solving (
7.2
) by encoding the problem in quantifier-free
linear real arithmetic
, though the encoding corresponds to an LP for queries without
\not\approx_{\epsilon}
.
The approach is summarized in
\Cref
alg:MOA.

Input:

Extended MOA query constructed by
\Cref
alg:conj

Input:
\displaystyle\exists\sigma\in\Sigma^{\mathcal{M}_{\mathscr{C}}}.\ \bigwedge_{j=1}^{l}\mathbb{E}_{s_{\mathscr{C}}}^{\mathcal{M}_{\mathscr{C}},\sigma}(\textit{R}^{j})\bowtie_{j}q_{j}

//
See
\Cref
le:reduce-to-moa

1

Output:

Whether the query is satisfiable

\overline{\mathcal{M}_{\mathscr{C}}}\leftarrow
pre-processing of
\mathcal{M}_{\mathscr{C}}

//
See
\Cref
def:conj_preprocessing

L\leftarrow
encoding from
\Cref
fig:LP

//
Potentially containing
\neq,\not\approx

return

Solve(
L
)

//
SMT solver or iterative calls to LP solvers (
\Cref
alg:LP)

Algorithm 5

Solving extended MOA queries for
MORelReach

Pre-processing.

Following
[
24
]
, we first process
\mathcal{M}_{\mathscr{C}}
to ensure that any scheduler witnessing the original query corresponds to a witness scheduler for the query on the processed MDP
\overline{\mathcal{M}_{\mathscr{C}}}
that visits all states from
\mathcal{M}_{\mathscr{C}}
in
\overline{\mathcal{M}_{\mathscr{C}}}
only finitely often, and vice versa.
Recall that
\mathcal{M}_{\mathscr{C}}
is already a goal unfolding, i.e., for any MEC in
\mathcal{M}_{\mathscr{C}}
there must exist some
\mathcal{T}
and
c\in\mathscr{C}
such that all states from the MEC are of the form
((s,\mathcal{T}),c)
for some
s\in S
.
Concretely, we construct
\overline{\mathcal{M}_{\mathscr{C}}}
from
\mathcal{M}_{\mathscr{C}}
by adding new sink states
\bot_{\mathcal{T}}
for every combination of target sets
\mathcal{T}
and adding transitions to
\bot_{\mathcal{T}}
via a fresh action
\dagger
from all states
s\in S_{\textit{MEC}}
that have already seen exactly the target sets from
\mathcal{T}
.
Intuitively, going to
\bot_{\mathcal{T}}
represents staying forever in the current MEC (and thus not visiting any more target sets).

Definition 76
.

Let
\mathcal{T}_{\mathscr{C}}:=\bigcup_{c\in\mathscr{C}_{\mathscr{C}}}\mathcal{T}_{c}
.
Given
\mathcal{M}_{\mathscr{C}}
as constructed in
\Cref
def:MDP_partelt, we construct its
pre-processing

\overline{\mathcal{M}_{\mathscr{C}}}=(\overline{S_{\mathscr{C}}},\allowbreak\overline{\textit{Act}_{\mathscr{C}}},\allowbreak\overline{\mathbf{P}_{\mathscr{C}}})
with

•

\overline{S_{\mathscr{C}}}=S_{\mathscr{C}}\cup\overbrace{\{\bot_{\mathcal{T}}\mid\mathcal{T}\subseteq\mathcal{T}_{\mathscr{C}}\}}^{S_{\bot}}
,

•

\overline{\textit{Act}_{\mathscr{C}}}=\textit{Act}_{\mathscr{C}}\cup\{\dagger\}
for a fresh action
\dagger
,

•

\overline{\mathbf{P}_{\mathscr{C}}}(s,\alpha,s^{\prime})=\mathbf{P}_{\mathscr{C}}(s,\alpha,s^{\prime})
for
s,s^{\prime}\in S_{\mathscr{C}}
,
\alpha\in\textit{Act}_{\mathscr{C}}
,

\overline{\mathbf{P}_{\mathscr{C}}}(s,\dagger,\bot_{\mathcal{T}})=1
for
s\in S_{\mathscr{C}}
and
\mathcal{T}\subseteq\mathcal{T}_{\mathscr{C}}
if there exists some
C=(T,A)\in\textit{MEC}(\mathcal{M}_{\mathscr{C}})
such that
s\in T
and there exist
s^{\prime}\in S
,
c\in\mathscr{C}_{\mathscr{C}}
such that
s=((s^{\prime},\mathcal{T}),c)
,

\overline{\mathbf{P}_{\mathscr{C}}}(\bot_{\mathcal{T}},\dagger,\bot_{\mathcal{T}})=1
for
\mathcal{T}\subseteq\mathcal{T}_{\mathscr{C}}
,
and all other transition probabilities are 0.

Example 77
.

Continuing our running example from
\Cref
ex:combined, the states and transitions added by the pre-processing of
\mathcal{M}_{\llbracket 1\rrbracket}
are indicated in
gray
in
\Cref
fig:conj_running.

If some scheduler for
\overline{\mathcal{M}_{\mathscr{C}}}
almost-surely reaches
S_{\bot}
, then the expected number of times we transition into or out of a state is finite for all states
s\in S_{\mathscr{C}}
in
\overline{\mathcal{M}_{\mathscr{C}}}
under that scheduler.
Hence, the pre-processing ensures that the expected number of times we leave a state
s
via an action
\alpha
is finite for all states from
\mathcal{M}_{\mathscr{C}}
and thus enables us to encode the problem using variables representing these values.
We show that finding a
memoryless
randomized scheduler for the pre-processed MDP that satisfies the extended MOA query and reaches
S_{\bot}
with probability 1 is equivalent to solving the original query.
This claim can be generalized to any MDP
\mathcal{M}
with reward functions
\textit{R}^{j}
whose expected reward is finite under all schedulers, analogously to
[
24
]
.
Our constructed MDP
\overline{\mathcal{M}_{\mathscr{C}}}
satisfies the stronger requirement that we collect finite reward
on all paths
under all schedulers, or, equivalently, that we collect non-zero reward only outside MECs.
We state the claim here for our special case only for the sake of a more stream-lined presentation

Theorem 78
.

Let
\overline{\mathcal{M}_{\mathscr{C}}}
as defined in
\Cref
def:conj_preprocessing with sink states
S_{\bot}
, then

\displaystyle\exists\sigma\in\Sigma^{\mathcal{M}_{\mathscr{C}}}.\ \bigwedge_{j=1}^{l}\mathbb{E}_{s_{\mathscr{C}}}^{\mathcal{M}_{\mathscr{C}},\sigma}(\textit{R}^{j})\bowtie_{j}q_{j}

\displaystyle\mathbin{\textit{ iff }}\quad

\displaystyle\exists\overline{\sigma}\in\Sigma^{\overline{\mathcal{M}_{\mathscr{C}}}}_{\textit{MR}}.\ \bigwedge_{j=1}^{l}\mathbb{E}_{s_{\mathscr{C}}}^{{\overline{\mathcal{M}_{\mathscr{C}}}},\overline{\sigma}}(\textit{R}^{j})\bowtie_{j}q_{j}\wedge{\text{Pr}}_{s_{\mathscr{C}}}^{{\overline{\mathcal{M}_{\mathscr{C}}}},\overline{\sigma}}(\operatorname{\hbox to8.7pt{\vbox to8.7pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 0.25833pt\lower-1.76527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@closescope \hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lxSVG@roundjoin\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-linejoin} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 3.57 L 5.66 9.23 L 11.32 3.57 L 5.66 -2.09 Z}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope }
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}S_{\bot})=1.

Proof (Sketch).

We show this claim in two steps, analogously to
[
24
]
.
First, we show that any scheduler for
\mathcal{M}_{\mathscr{C}}
induces a scheduler for
\overline{\mathcal{M}_{\mathscr{C}}}
that reaches
S_{\bot}
with probability 1, and vice versa.
Then we show that memoryless (randomized) schedulers suffice on
\overline{\mathcal{M}_{\mathscr{C}}}
.
We refer to
\Cref
app:moa_preproc_transfer-MR_proof for the proof, which uses ideas from
[
39
,
24
]
.
∎

Example 79
.

Consider again the running example introduced in
\Cref
ex:conj_running and the corresponding pre-processed MDP, depicted in
\Cref
fig:conj_running.
Following
\Cref

\displaystyle\exists\sigma\in\Sigma^{\mathcal{M}_{\llbracket 1\rrbracket}}.\ \mathbb{E}_{s_{\llbracket 1\rrbracket}}^{\mathcal{M}_{\llbracket 1\rrbracket},\sigma}(\textit{R}^{1})<0\land\mathbb{E}_{s_{\llbracket 1\rrbracket}}^{\mathcal{M}_{\llbracket 1\rrbracket},\sigma}(\textit{R}^{2})>0

\displaystyle\mathbin{\textit{ iff }}\quad

\displaystyle\exists\sigma\in\Sigma^{\overline{\mathcal{M}_{\llbracket 1\rrbracket}}}.\ \mathbb{E}_{s_{\llbracket 1\rrbracket}}^{\mathcal{M}_{\llbracket 1\rrbracket},\sigma}(\textit{R}^{1})<0\land\mathbb{E}_{s_{\llbracket 1\rrbracket}}^{\mathcal{M}_{\llbracket 1\rrbracket},\sigma}(\textit{R}^{2})>0\land{\text{Pr}}_{s_{\llbracket 1\rrbracket}}^{\mathcal{M}_{\llbracket 1\rrbracket},\sigma}(\operatorname{\hbox to8.7pt{\vbox to8.7pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 0.25833pt\lower-1.76527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@closescope \hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lxSVG@roundjoin\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-linejoin} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 3.57 L 5.66 9.23 L 11.32 3.57 L 5.66 -2.09 Z}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces

.

Encoding in linear real arithmetic.

\Cref

fig:LP encodes the query (
7.2
) in quantifier-free linear real arithmetic, analogously to
[
24
, Fig. 2]
, where we introduce
\not\approx_{\epsilon}
as syntactic sugar.
The encoding uses variables
y_{s,\alpha}
for
s\in S_{\mathscr{C}}
,
\alpha\in\overline{\textit{Act}_{\mathscr{C}}}(s)
encoding the expected number of times we leave
s
via
\alpha
.
Constraint (
7.3
) intuitively expresses that for
s\in S_{\mathscr{C}}
, the expected number of times we leave
s
corresponds to the expected number of times we enter
s
, plus 1 if
s
is the initial state
s_{\mathscr{C}}
.
Constraint (
7.4
) expresses the extended MOA query and (
7.5
) expresses the added constraint of reaching
S_{\bot}
with probability 1.
For a property without
\not\approx_{\epsilon}
, this corresponds to an LP feasibility problem.
We can use ideas from
[
22
,
24
]
to show the correctness of the encoding, see
\Cref
app:conj_LP_proof.

Find assignments for variables

y_{s,\alpha}\text{ for }s\in S_{\mathscr{C}},\alpha\in\overline{\textit{Act}_{\mathscr{C}}}(s)

such that

\displaystyle\sum_{\alpha\in\overline{\textit{Act}_{\mathscr{C}}}(s)}\hskip-8.61108pty_{s,\alpha}-\hskip-4.30554pt\sum_{s^{\prime}\in S_{\mathscr{C}}}\sum_{\alpha^{\prime}\in\textit{Act}_{\mathscr{C}}(s^{\prime})}\hskip-8.61108pt\mathbf{P}_{\mathscr{C}}(s^{\prime},\alpha^{\prime},s)\cdot y_{s^{\prime},\alpha^{\prime}}

\displaystyle=[s{=}s_{\mathscr{C}}]

\displaystyle\text{for }s\in S_{\mathscr{C}}

(7.3)

\displaystyle\sum_{s\in S_{\mathscr{C}}}\hskip-4.30554pt\textit{R}^{j}(s)\cdot\sum_{s^{\prime}\in S_{\mathscr{C}}}\sum_{\alpha^{\prime}\in\textit{Act}_{\mathscr{C}}(s^{\prime})}\hskip-8.61108pt\mathbf{P}_{\mathscr{C}}(s^{\prime},\alpha^{\prime},s)\cdot y_{s^{\prime},\alpha^{\prime}}

\displaystyle\bowtie_{j}q_{j}

\displaystyle\text{for }j=1,\ldots,l

(7.4)

\displaystyle\sum_{s\in(S_{\mathscr{C}})_{\textit{MEC}}}y_{s,\dagger}

\displaystyle=1

(7.5)

\displaystyle y_{s,\alpha}

\displaystyle\geq 0

\displaystyle\text{for }s\in S_{\mathscr{C}},\alpha\in\overline{\textit{Act}_{\mathscr{C}}}(s)

(7.6)

Figure 23:
Linear real arithmetic encoding for extended MOA queries with
\bowtie_{j}\in\{>,\geq,\approx_{\epsilon},\not\approx_{\epsilon}\mid\epsilon\geq 0\}
, where we use
x\not\approx_{\epsilon}y
as syntactic sugar for
x<y-\epsilon\lor x>y+\epsilon
.
Recall that
\overline{S_{\mathscr{C}}}=S_{\mathscr{C}}\cup S_{\bot}
and that
S_{\bot}
consists of sink states that can only be reached via
\dagger
, i.e.,
\overline{\mathbf{P}_{\mathscr{C}}}(s^{\prime},\dagger,s)=0
for
s,s^{\prime}\in S_{\mathscr{C}}
.

Lemma 81
.

Let
\overline{\mathcal{M}_{\mathscr{C}}}
as defined in
\Cref
def:MDP_combined, then

\displaystyle\exists\overline{\sigma}\in\Sigma^{\overline{\mathcal{M}_{\mathscr{C}}}}_{\textit{MR}}.\ \bigwedge_{j=1}^{l}\mathbb{E}_{s_{\mathscr{C}}}^{{\overline{\mathcal{M}_{\mathscr{C}}}},\overline{\sigma}}(\textit{R}^{j})\bowtie_{j}q_{j}\wedge{\text{Pr}}_{s_{\mathscr{C}}}^{{\overline{\mathcal{M}_{\mathscr{C}}}},\overline{\sigma}}(\operatorname{\hbox to8.7pt{\vbox to8.7pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 0.25833pt\lower-1.76527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@closescope \hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lxSVG@roundjoin\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-linejoin} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 3.57 L 5.66 9.23 L 11.32 3.57 L 5.66 -2.09 Z}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope }
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}S_{\bot})=1

\displaystyle\mathbin{\textit{ iff }}\quad

the encoding from
\Cref
fig:LP has a feasible solution
y^{*}
.

Due to the presence of
\approx_{\epsilon}
-,
>
-, and
\not\approx_{\epsilon}
-constraints, the constructed encoding in linear real arithmetic does not necessarily correspond to a linear program, whose standard form only allows
\leq,\geq
-constraints.
While
\approx_{\epsilon}
- and
>
-constraints can be transformed to
\leq,\geq
-constraints (see, e.g.,
[
21
,
44
]
for transforming
>
to
\geq
), the situation is more complex for
\not\approx_{\epsilon}
.
A linear real arithmetic encoding containing
\not\approx_{\epsilon}
can be solved using successive calls to an LP solver, in the worst case exponentially many, while in the special case of
\not\approx_{0}
, i.e.,
\neq
, we only need to solve a
linear
number of calls to an LP solver, see
\Cref
sec:moa_via_lp for details.

Overall algorithm.

The algorithm resulting from the approach outlined above is summarized in
\Cref
alg:MOA.
It has a worst-case running time exponential in the number of probability operators (even without
\not\approx
).
We note that while this approach gives us a tighter upper bound for the complexity of MOA than a Pareto-curve-based approach (which would be exponential also in the size of the MDP), in practice, an approximate Pareto-curve-based approach may significantly outperform our exact approach, see, e.g.,
[
25
]
.

Theorem 82
.

\Cref

alg:MOA adheres to its input-output specification and can be implemented with worst-case running time of
\mathcal{O}(2^{l_{\not\approx}}\cdot\textit{poly}(l\cdot m\cdot|\mathcal{M}|\cdot 2^{l\cdot m}))

where
l
is the number of predicates,
l_{\not\approx}
the number of predicates with
\not\approx_{\epsilon}
with
\epsilon>0
, and
m
the number of probability operators per objective in the original
MORelReach
property, i.e., the original input to
\Cref
alg:conj.

Proof.

Correctness follows from
\Cref
le:conj_LP and
\Cref
th:moa_preproc_transfer-MR.

If the extended MOA query does not contain
\not\approx_{\epsilon}
with
\epsilon>0
, then the encoding is a quantifier-free formula with a conjunction over at most
|S_{\mathscr{C}}|+l+1+|S_{\mathscr{C}}|\cdot|\overline{\textit{Act}_{\mathscr{C}}}|
linear real constraints.
Observe

|S_{\mathscr{C}}|\cdot|\overline{\textit{Act}_{\mathscr{C}}}|\leq(l\cdot m\cdot|S|\cdot 2^{l\cdot m}+1)\cdot(|\textit{Act}|+2)
.
The claim then follows since quantifier-free linear real arithmetic with only conjunctions can be solved in time polynomial in the number of constraints
[
37
]
.
In particular, if
\neq
-predicates are present, it suffices to first check the encoding without the
\neq
-constraints—which is an LP—and then checking each
\neq
-constraint one by one
[
41
, Th. 1]
.
Otherwise, if the extended MOA query contains
l_{\not\approx}\leq l
constraints with
\not\approx_{\epsilon}
with
\epsilon>0
, then the encoding corresponds to a disjunction over
2^{l_{\not\approx}}
quantifier-free formulas with only conjunctions over linear real constraints without
\not\approx_{\epsilon}
with
\epsilon>0
.
We can solve this disjunction by iterating over the disjuncts and solving each disjunct separately.
See
\Cref
sec:moa_via_lp for details.
∎

7.2
Complexity of Multi-Objective Relational Reachability

Since already
MORelReach
with a single predicate is
PSPACE
-hard, the problem with an arbitrary number of objectives is
PSPACE
-hard as well.

Theorem 83
.

Problem
MORelReach
is
PSPACE
-hard and decidable in
EXPTIME
.

Proof.

PSPACE
-hardness follows from the fact that the problem is already
PSPACE
-hard for a single predicate
\Cref
th:general_EXPTIME, no matter whether we include
\not\approx_{\epsilon}
with
\epsilon>0
or not.
Membership in
EXPTIME
follows from
\Cref
th:conj_runtime.
∎

The exact complexity of
MORelReach
remains open for reasons similar to those discussed after the proof of
\Cref
th:general_EXPTIME.

Recall the
PTIME
-solvable special cases of
RelReach
from
\Cref
th:general_PTIME: a constant number of target sets, all target sets absorbing, and properties where each probability operator has its own quantifier.
Multi-objective relational properties without
\not\approx_{\epsilon}
(
\epsilon>0
) remain in
PTIME
in these cases.
However, if the property contains at least one
\not\approx_{\epsilon}
with
\epsilon>0
, some of these special cases become (strongly)
NP
-complete:

Theorem 84
.

Consider the following special cases of
MORelReach
, where
l_{\not\approx}
denotes the number of predicates with
\not\approx_{\epsilon}
with
\epsilon>0
:

l_{\not\approx}>0

l_{\not\approx}=0

(a)

|\{T_{1,1},\ldots,T_{m,l}\}|
is at most a constant

strongly
NP
-complete

PTIME

(b)

all target sets are absorbing

strongly
NP
-complete

PTIME

(c)

n=m\cdot l
, i.e., each probability operator

has its own quantifier

PTIME

PTIME

Proof.

\ref{item:conj_fixed-param},\ref{item:conj_absorb}
:
MORelReach
is
NP
-hard by reduction from 3SAT to a
MORelReach
property of the form

\exists\sigma_{1},\ldots,\sigma_{n}.\ \bigwedge_{j=1}^{n}{\text{Pr}}^{\sigma_{j}}_{s}(\operatorname{\hbox to8.7pt{\vbox to8.7pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 0.25833pt\lower-1.76527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@closescope \hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lxSVG@roundjoin\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-linejoin} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 3.57 L 5.66 9.23 L 11.32 3.57 L 5.66 -2.09 Z}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope }
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}T)\not\approx_{\epsilon}q_{j}~,

i.e., a property with a single, absorbing target set shared by all probability operators.
The reduction can be found in
\Cref
app:conj_approx-diseq_NP.

Membership in
NP
/
PTIME
: If the number of different target sets is at most a constant or all targets are absorbing, then the size of the goal unfolding
\mathcal{M}_{c}
is polynomial for each
c\in\mathscr{C}
and thus also the size of
\mathcal{M}_{\mathscr{C}}
is polynomial.
Hence, the encoding from
\Cref
fig:LP has a polynomial number of variables and constraints.
Thus, we can guess a feasible solution
y^{*}
, whose size is polynomial in the size of the MDP, and verify whether it is indeed a solution in polynomial time.
In particular, if the property does not contain
\not\approx_{\epsilon}
with
\epsilon>0
, we can even solve the encoding in
PTIME
following the reasoning from
\Cref
th:MOA_correctness.

84
: If
n=m\cdot l
, then no predicates share schedulers, so we can solve each predicate separately. We thus have to solve
l
independent
RelReach
properties where each probability operator has its own scheduler, which can be solved in
PTIME
by
\Cref
th:general_PTIME
(c)
. ∎

In contrast to previous sections, we do not study cases where the model-checking problem under memoryless deterministic schedulers,
MORelReach
{}_{\textsf{MD}}
, is in
PTIME
, but only make the following two statements that follow directly from
\Cref
th:MD_NP-complete
5
5

5

To be precise, membership in
NP
for
MORelReach
{}_{\textsf{MD}}
does not follow directly from
\Cref
th:MD_NP-complete but with analogous reasoning.
and
\Cref
th:general_necessary.
In order to analyze fragments where our algorithm returns MD schedulers in polynomial time, we would need to analyze in which cases MD schedulers suffice for multi-objective achievability queries and can be computed in
PTIME
, which we consider out of scope here.
While the complexity of solving MOA queries over MD schedulers has been studied before
[
17
,
12
,
57
,
55
]
,
we are not aware of work analyzing cases where MD schedulers suffice for MOA queries.

Corollary 85
.

MORelReach
{}_{\textsf{MD}}
is strongly
NP
-complete.

Corollary 86
.

Memory and randomization are necessary for
MORelReach
with (approximate or exact) equality.

Summary of
\Cref
sec:conjunction

The key insight is that
MORelReach
can be reduced to multi-objective achievability queries with expected reward objectives.
Even though the constructed reward structures assign both positive and negative reward, we can solve the constructed query in time exponential in the number of probability operators of the property by extending existing approaches
[
22
,
24
]
.

MORelReach
is in general
PSPACE
-hard.
We investigate the complexity of
MORelReach
in the analogous case to those where
RelReach
can be solved in
PTIME
and observe that
MORelReach
without
\not\approx_{\epsilon}
for
\epsilon>0
can also be solved in
PTIME
in these cases, but is strongly
NP
-complete in some of the cases if there are predicates
\not\approx_{\epsilon}
for
\epsilon>0
.

MORelReach
{}_{\textsf{MD}}
is strongly
NP
-complete.

8
Implementation and Evaluation

We have implemented model-checking algorithms for
RelReach
and
RelBuechi
based on the procedures described in
\Cref
sec:buechi_algo,sec:conj_algo.
Extending our implementation to
MORelReach
is current work;
we believe that the current results already give a good insight into our approach’s level of applicability.
We use our prototypical implementation to investigate the scalability of our approach in terms of model size, and how it performs compared to existing tools that can check relational properties.
We give details on our setup in
\Cref
sec:setup, and report on results for evaluating our prototypical implementation on case studies for relational properties (
\Cref
sec:case-studies_new) as well as benchmarks for probabilistic hyperproperties (
\Cref
sec:hyperprob-benchmarks).
The experiments on relational reachability probabilities are similar to the ones in
[
28
]
, but have been run on the latest version of our software.

8.1
Setup

All experiments were performed on a laptop with a single core of a
1.80
GHz Intel i7 CPU and 16GB RAM under Linux Ubuntu 24.04 LTS.

Implementation.

Our prototype
6
6

6

Source code and benchmarks:
https://github.com/carolinager/RelProp/releases/tag/RelReachBuechi
, artifact with scripts to reproduce experiments:
https://doi.org/10.5281/zenodo.19450015
is implemented on top of (the python bindings of) the probabilistic model checker
\storm

[
32
]
and supports an expressive fragment of relational properties. It takes as input an MDP (in the form of a
PRISM
file
[
40
]
) with
m
(not necessarily distinct) initial states and
m
(not necessarily distinct) target sets,
as well as a
universally quantified
relational property, i.e., the negation of a relational formula as defined in
\Cref
sec:problem-statement.
More specifically, our prototype covers universally quantified relational reachability properties, and universally quantified relational Büchi properties.

Most available case studies for relational properties are universally quantified.
Recall that, while the previous sections addressed existentially quantified relational properties, we can transfer our results to universally quantified relational properties by considering their negation.

Note that
the tool’s output (‘Yes’/‘No’) depends only on comparing an expected reward to a threshold (
\Cref
alg:linear_general_simplified,
\Cref
line:linear_general_simplified_return), so the same calculations are performed regardless of the comparison’s outcome.
Hence, performance is generally unaffected by an instance’s falsifiability in these cases.

We use
\storm
’s internal single- and multi-objective model checking capabilities for the computation of expected rewards (
\Cref
alg:linear_general_simplified,
\Cref
line:linear_general_simplified_step3),

where single-objective model checking employs
Optimistic Value Iteration

[
31
]
for approximate expected reward computation with a default tolerance of
10^{-6}
.
7
7

7

Note that
\storm
returns a single value
x
with relative difference at most
10^{-6}
to the exact result instead of sound lower and upper bounds here. We ensure soundness by computing conservative lower and upper bounds as
\tfrac{x}{1+10^{-6}}
and
\tfrac{x}{1-10^{-6}}
.

The multi-objective model checking employs
Sound Value Iteration

[
49
]
with the same tolerance.
8
8

8

\storm
(currently) returns a single value with relative difference at most
10^{-6}
to the exact result as both lower and upper bound and we again ensure soundness manually.

Here, we report results for approximate computation of expected rewards with tolerance
10^{-6}
.

While parts of this section already appeared in
[
28
]
, we performed all experiments again with our updated prototype, which is based on a newer version of
\storm
compared to the artifact provided for
[
28
]
.

Baseline.

We compare our tool against two baselines:

HyperProb

9
9

9

https://github.com/TART-MSU/HyperProb

[
19
]
and
HyperPAYNT

10
10

10

https://github.com/probing-lab/HyperPAYNT
, we used this docker container:
https://zenodo.org/records/8116528
. Also referred to as
“AR loop”
in
[
6
]
.

[
6
]
.
These tools handle (fragments of much more expressive) probabilistic hyperproperties that partially overlap with relational properties.

HyperProb
encodes the
HyperPCTL
model checking problem in SMT with an exponential number of variables.

HyperPAYNT
uses abstraction refinement to model-check a fragment of
HyperPCTL
, potentially exploring an exponential number of schedulers.
To the best of our knowledge, no other tools support a (nontrivial) fragment of relational properties.

HyperProb
and
HyperPAYNT
support approximate comparison operators via an equivalent conjunction or disjunction of two inequalities.
11
11

11

While not covered in
[
6
]
,
HyperPAYNT
also allows to add a constant to one side of the (in)equality.

HyperPAYNT
restricts to reachability objectives, it does not cover Büchi objectives.
While
HyperPCTL
does not allow direct nesting of temporal operators, Büchi probabilities are
PCTL
-definable via
\mathbb{P}(\operatorname{\hbox to6.98pt{\vbox to6.98pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 0.25833pt\lower-1.11945pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@closescope \hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {

\lxSVG@closescope }
