In order to show
\Cref
th:relBuechi-to-relReach_Leftarrow using
\Cref
th:relBuechi-to-relReach_Leftarrow_MR, we define the
flip-extension
of an MDP
\mathcal{M}
with some initial state
s_{0}\in S
, which encodes the memory structure of schedulers that are the convex combination of two schedulers for
\mathcal{M}
into the MDP.

Definition 91
.

Given an MDP
\mathcal{M}=(S,\allowbreak\textit{Act},\allowbreak\mathbf{P})
and
s_{0}\in S
, the
flip-extension
of
\mathcal{M}
w.r.t.
s_{0}
is defined as
{\mathcal{M}}_{\circlearrowright s_{0}}=({S}_{\circlearrowright s_{0}},\allowbreak{\textit{Act}}_{\circlearrowright s_{0}},\allowbreak{\mathbf{P}}_{\circlearrowright s_{0}})
with

•

{S}_{\circlearrowright s_{0}}=(S\times\{1\})\cup(\{S\}\times\{2\})\cup\{s_{\textit{flip}}\}
,

•

{\textit{Act}}_{\circlearrowright s_{0}}=\textit{Act}\cup\{1,2\}
, and

•

{\mathbf{P}}_{\circlearrowright s_{0}}\colon{S}_{\circlearrowright s_{0}}\times{\textit{Act}}_{\circlearrowright s_{0}}\times{S}_{\circlearrowright s_{0}}\to[0,1]
is defined by case distinction:

{\mathbf{P}}_{\circlearrowright s_{0}}(s_{\textit{flip}},i,(s_{0},i))=1
for
i\in\{1,2\}
,

{\mathbf{P}}_{\circlearrowright s_{0}}((s,i),\alpha,(s^{\prime},i))=\mathbf{P}(s,\alpha,s^{\prime})
for
i\in\{1,2\}
and
s,s^{\prime}\in S
and
\alpha\in\textit{Act}
,

and all other transition probabilities are 0.

Hence, every scheduler for
{\mathcal{M}}_{\circlearrowright s_{0}}
can directly be interpreted as the convex combination of two schedulers for
\mathcal{M}
, and vice versa.
In particular, memoryless randomized schedulers suffice on
{\mathcal{M}}_{\circlearrowright s_{0}}
to achieve any value for a weighted sum of reachability probabilities that is achievable on
\mathcal{M}
:

Lemma 92
.

Let
c\in\mathscr{C}
and
q_{c}\in\mathbb{Q}
, then

\displaystyle\exists\widehat{\sigma}\in\Sigma^{\widehat{\mathcal{M}}}.\ \sum_{j\in\mathit{ind}(c)}q_{j}{\text{Pr}}^{\widehat{\mathcal{M}},\widehat{\sigma}}_{f(s_{c})}\left(\operatorname{\hbox to8.7pt{\vbox to8.7pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 0.25833pt\lower-1.76527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@closescope \hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lxSVG@roundjoin\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-linejoin} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 3.57 L 5.66 9.23 L 11.32 3.57 L 5.66 -2.09 Z}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope }
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}U_{T_{j}}\right)=q_{c}

\displaystyle\mathbin{\textit{ iff }}\quad

\displaystyle\exists\widehat{\sigma}^{\prime}\in\Sigma^{{(\widehat{\mathcal{M}})}_{\circlearrowright f(s_{c})}}_{\textit{MR}}.\ \sum_{j\in\mathit{ind}(c)}q_{j}{\text{Pr}}^{{(\widehat{\mathcal{M}})}_{\circlearrowright f(s_{c})},\widehat{\sigma}^{\prime}}_{s_{\circlearrowright}}\left(\operatorname{\hbox to8.7pt{\vbox to8.7pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 0.25833pt\lower-1.76527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@closescope \hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lxSVG@roundjoin\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-linejoin} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 3.57 L 5.66 9.23 L 11.32 3.57 L 5.66 -2.09 Z}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope }
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}U_{T_{j}}\right)=q_{c}

Proof.

First observe that for
i=1,2
, any scheduler for the
S\times\{i\}
-part of
(\widehat{\mathcal{M}})_{\textit{flip}@f(s_{j})}
can be interpreted as a scheduler for
\widehat{\mathcal{M}}
, and vice versa.

‘
\Leftarrow
’:
Let
\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2}\in\Sigma^{\widehat{\mathcal{M}}}
be the schedulers induced by
\widehat{\sigma}^{\prime}\in\Sigma^{{(\widehat{\mathcal{M}})}_{\circlearrowright f(s_{j})}}_{\textit{MR}}
on the
1
- and
2
-copy of
\mathcal{M}
.
We define
\widehat{\sigma}\in\Sigma^{\widehat{\mathcal{M}}}
as the convex combination of
\sigma_{1}
and
\sigma_{2}
w.r.t.
\lambda=\widehat{\sigma}^{\prime}(s_{\circlearrowright},1)
.

‘
\Rightarrow
’:
By construction, the sets
U_{T_{j}}
contain only absorbing states, i.e.,
\widehat{\mathcal{M}}
is already a goal unfolding w.r.t. these sets.
Following the construction from
\Cref
sec:reach_algo, we can thus reduce the
RelReach
query
\exists\sigma^{\prime}\in\Sigma^{\widehat{\mathcal{M}}}.\ \sum_{j\in\mathit{ind}(c)}q_{j}{\text{Pr}}^{\widehat{\mathcal{M}},\sigma^{\prime}}_{f(s_{0})}(\operatorname{\hbox to8.7pt{\vbox to8.7pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 0.25833pt\lower-1.76527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@closescope \hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lxSVG@roundjoin\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-linejoin} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 3.57 L 5.66 9.23 L 11.32 3.57 L 5.66 -2.09 Z}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope }
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}U_{T_{j}})=x
to a single-objective expected reward query on
\widehat{\mathcal{M}}
itself such that the expected reward under some scheduler equals exactly the value of the weighted sum of reachability probabilities under that scheduler.
There exist memoryless deterministic schedulers minimizing and maximizing the expected reward, respectively (see, e.g.,
[
46
, Th. 7.1.9]
).
Since
x
is achievable (via witness
\widehat{\sigma}
), there must exist some
\lambda\in[0,1]
such that the convex combination of the minimizing and the maximizing scheduler w.r.t.
\lambda
achieves exactly expected reward
x
.
We construct
\widehat{\sigma}^{\prime}\in\Sigma^{{(\widehat{\mathcal{M}})}_{\circlearrowright f(s_{j})}}_{\textit{MR}}

by choosing action
1
in
s_{\circlearrowright}
with probability
\lambda
, behaving like the maximizing scheduler in the
1
-copy of
\widehat{\mathcal{M}}
and like the minimizing scheduler in the
2
-copy of
\widehat{\mathcal{M}}
.
∎

We are now ready to prove
\Cref
th:relBuechi-to-relReach_Leftarrow.

Proof of
\Cref
th:relBuechi-to-relReach_Leftarrow.

We reduce both sides of the implication from
\Cref
th:relBuechi-to-relReach_Leftarrow_MR to reasoning about schedulers for the flip-extensions of the respective MDPs, which then allows us to conclude the claim by applying
\Cref
th:relBuechi-to-relReach_Leftarrow_MR.

Firstly, observe that the sets
U_{T_{j}}
are absorbing and hence any value achievable for the weighted sum
\sum_{j\in\mathit{ind}(c)}q_{j}{\text{Pr}}^{\widehat{\mathcal{M}},\widehat{\sigma}}_{f(s_{c})}\left(\operatorname{\hbox to8.7pt{\vbox to8.7pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 0.25833pt\lower-1.76527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@closescope \hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lxSVG@roundjoin\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-linejoin} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 3.57 L 5.66 9.23 L 11.32 3.57 L 5.66 -2.09 Z}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope }
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}U_{T_{j}}\right)
can be achieved by the convex combination of two MD schedulers (see proof of
\Cref

Thus, any value achievable for the weighted sum of reachability probabilities on the quotient can be achieved by a
memoryless
randomized scheduler on the flip-extension of the quotient, and vice versa.
For
c\in\mathscr{C}
, let

be the unique state in
\mathcal{M}
with
s_{j}=s_{c}
for all
j\in\mathit{ind}(c)
.

(1)

\displaystyle\mathbin{\textit{ iff }}\quad

The claim ‘
(

)
implies
(
2’
)
’ is an instance of
\Cref

and

Appendix E
Proofs for
\Cref
sec:buechi_complexity

E.1
Proof of
\Cref
th:buechi_NP-complete

See
49

Proof.

We first show membership in
NP
and then strong
NP
-hardness.

\proofsubparagraph

Membership in
NP
:
Given some MDP
\mathcal{M}
and
RelBuechi
property
\varphi
, let
\varphi^{\prime}
be the constructed
RelReach
property.
We will show that it suffices to guess a polynomial number of MD schedulers in order to guess a witness for the query.

The key observation is that all target sets
U_{T_{i}}
of
\varphi^{\prime}
on
\widehat{\mathcal{M}}
are absorbing and therefore for each
c\in\mathscr{C}
, the goal unfolding of
\widehat{\mathcal{M}}
w.r.t. the target sets for
c
,
\mathcal{T}_{c}^{\prime}=\{U_{T_{i}}\mid i\in\mathit{ind}(c)\}
, corresponds to
\widehat{\mathcal{M}}
with an additional copy of each sink in some
U_{T_{i}}
for some
i\in\mathit{ind}(c)
.
An MD scheduler for
(\widehat{\mathcal{M}})_{c}
can thus be translated to an MD scheduler for
\widehat{\mathcal{M}}
while preserving the reachability probabilities.
By
\Cref
th:relBuechi-to-relReach_Leftarrow_MD, an MD witness for
\varphi^{\prime}
on
\widehat{\mathcal{M}}
can be translated to an MD witness for
\varphi
on
\mathcal{M}
.

We distinguish by comparison operator:

•

Assume
\bowtie
is
\geq
or
>
.
Let us first assume there is only a single state-scheduler combination
\{c\}=\mathscr{C}
.
By
\Cref
th:relBuechi-to-relReach and
\Cref
thm:correctness, solving
\varphi
reduces to maximizing some expected reward on
(\widehat{\mathcal{M}})_{c}
, for which memoryless deterministic schedulers suffice.
By the above reasoning, an MD witness on
(\widehat{\mathcal{M}})_{c}
can be translated to an MD witness on
\mathcal{M}
.

For
\geq,>
-queries with
|\mathscr{C}|>1
, we can thus guess witnesses by guessing an MD witness for each state-scheduler combination.

•

Assume
\bowtie
is
\approx_{\epsilon}
.
Let us first assume there is only a single state-scheduler combination
\{c\}=\mathscr{C}
.
By
\Cref
th:relBuechi-to-relReach and
\Cref
thm:cruxApproxEqual, solving
\varphi
reduces to both maximizing and minimizing some expected reward on
(\widehat{\mathcal{M}})_{c}
, and checking whether the given bound
q
has distance at least
\epsilon
to the minimal and maximal achievable values.
With analogous reasoning to
\geq,>
, we can translate the maximizing and the minimizing schedulers to MD schedulers on
\mathcal{M}
.
We can thus guess a witness for
\varphi
by guessing an MD scheduler maximizing the expected reward and guessing an MD scheduler minimizing the expected reward.

For
\approx_{\epsilon}
with
|\mathscr{C}|>1
, we can thus guess witnesses by guessing two MD witnesses for each state-scheduler combination.

•

Assume
\bowtie
is
\not\approx_{\epsilon}
; recall that
\varphi
corresponds to a disjunction of two
RelReach
properties with comparison operators
<
and
>
, respectively.
We can guess a witness for
\varphi
by guessing which disjunct holds and then guessing an MD witness following the reasoning for
\geq,>
-queries.

Note that the above does not imply that MD schedulers suffice for
RelBuechi
, since
\Cref
thm:whyComb only holds for
memoryful
schedulers: The existence of an MD witness schedulers for each state-scheduler combination does not necessarily imply the existence of MD witnesses for the schedulers quantified in the original property (where several state-scheduler combinations may share a scheduler).

\proofsubparagraph

Strong
NP
-hardness:
We show
NP
-hardness by reduction from SAT
[
36
]
.
Given a SAT formula
\varphi
over variables
x_{1},\ldots,x_{N}
in conjunctive normal form with clauses
C_{1},\ldots,C_{M}\subseteq\{x_{i},\overline{x_{i}}\mid i\in\{1,\ldots,N\}\}
.
We construct the MDP
\mathcal{M}_{\varphi}=(s_{\varphi},\textit{Act}_{\varphi},\mathbf{P}_{\varphi})
, depicted in
\Cref
fig:buechi_NP-complete, with

•

S_{\varphi}=\{x_{i},\overline{x_{i}}\mid i\in\{1,\ldots,N\}\}

•

\textit{Act}_{\varphi}=\{\alpha,\overline{\alpha}\}

•

\mathbf{P}_{\varphi}(x_{i},\alpha,x_{i+1})=1
, and

\mathbf{P}_{\varphi}(x_{i},\overline{\alpha},\overline{x_{i+1}})=1
for
i\in\{1,\ldots,N-1\}
,

\mathbf{P}_{\varphi}(x_{N},\alpha,x_{1})=1
, and

\mathbf{P}_{\varphi}(x_{N},\overline{\alpha},\overline{x_{1}})=1
,
and all other transition probabilities are 0.

We construct the following
RelBuechi
property:

\displaystyle\exists\sigma\in\Sigma^{\mathcal{M}_{\varphi}}.\ \sum_{i=1}^{N}\left[2-({\text{Pr}}^{\sigma}_{x_{1}}(\operatorname{\hbox to6.98pt{\vbox to6.98pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 0.25833pt\lower-1.11945pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@closescope \hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lxSVG@roundjoin\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-linejoin} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 -1.19 L 0 7.74 L 8.94 7.74 L 8.94 -1.19 Z}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope }
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}\operatorname{\hbox to8.7pt{\vbox to8.7pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 0.25833pt\lower-1.76527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@closescope \hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}

(
\varphi^{\prime}
)

Let us now show that
\varphi
is satisfiable iff (

φ
′

) holds.

“
\Rightarrow
”: Assume
\varphi
is satisfiable with model
\mathcal{I}
. Then
\mathcal{I}
induces a memoryless deterministic scheduler as follows: If
\mathcal{I}
chooses
x_{i}
, we choose
\alpha
in
x_{i-1}
(modulo
N
), if
\mathcal{I}
chooses
\overline{x_{i}}
we choose
\overline{\alpha}
.

“
\Leftarrow
”: Assume (

φ
′

) holds.
Observe that
deterministic
schedulers suffice for satisfying this property:
By
\Cref
th:relBuechi-to-relReach,thm:correctness we can reduce this property to computing a scheduler maximizing some expected reward on the MEC quotient of
\mathcal{M}_{\varphi}
; for this memoryless deterministic schedulers suffice with analogous reasoning to
\Cref
thm:exRewPtimeMD and by
\Cref
th:relBuechi-to-relReach_Leftarrow_MD any MD scheduler on the goal unfolding can be translated back to an MD scheduler on the original MDP.

So let
\sigma
be a deterministic witness to (

φ
′

).

.

Claim:

The above construction defines a pseudo-polynomial time transformation.

The constructed MDP has
2\cdot N
states. Each state has at exactly two successors.
All transition probabilities in the MDP are either 0 or 1.
Hence, the magnitude of the largest number occurring in the constructed MDP is a constant.
The constructed
RelBuechi
property sums over
2N+M
probability operators, and contains coefficients
2
and
N+M
.
Hence, the magnitude of the largest number in the constructed
RelBuechi
instance is polynomial in the size of the original SAT instance.
∎

x_{1}
\overline{x_{1}}
x_{2}
\overline{x_{2}}
…
…
x_{N}
\overline{x_{N}}

Figure 31:
Illustration of the MDP construction for the reduction from SAT to
RelBuechi
.

E.2
Proof of
\Cref
th:buechi_general_PTIME

See
50

Proof.

(a)
: Follows from
\Cref
th:buechi_correctness.
Also, for a fixed
m
, the transformation from
RelBuechi
to
RelReach
given in
\Cref
sec:buechi_algo is a polynomial-time transformation. Hence, for any fixed
m
,
RelBuechi
with
m
target sets is Turing-reducible to
RelReach
with
m
target sets.

(b)
: If all targets are absorbing, then reaching a target is equivalent to visiting it infinitely often. Hence, a
RelBuechi
query can be restated as a
RelReach
query, which is in
PTIME
in this case due to
\Cref
th:general_PTIME
(b)
.

(c)
:
For every state-scheduler combination, there is a unique target set, so the size of the MEC quotient is linear in the size of the original MDP and hence the procedure detailed above runs in polynomial time.
∎

E.3
Proof of
\Cref
th:buechi_MD_NP-complete

See
52

Proof.

Membership in
NP
:
Given some
RelBuechi
property, MDP
\mathcal{M}
and a memoryless deterministic scheduler
\sigma\in\Sigma^{\mathcal{M}}
, we can verify whether
\sigma
is a witness for the property
by computing the (exact) probabilities of the Büchi objectives in the induced DTMC.
This is possible in time polynomial in the size of the state space
[
11
]
.

NP
-Hardness: We can show
NP
-hardness by reduction from the Hamiltonian path problem as in the proof of
\Cref
th:MD_NP-complete.
For a given instance of the Hamiltonian path problem
G=(V,E)
and
v_{\textit{init}}\in V
, we construct the MDP
\mathcal{M}
as before.
Observe that both target states
s_{a}
and
s_{b}
are absorbing, hence for any scheduler
\sigma\in\Sigma^{\mathcal{M}}
we have

{\text{Pr}}^{\sigma}_{s_{0}}(\operatorname{\hbox to8.7pt{\vbox to8.7pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 0.25833pt\lower-1.76527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@closescope \hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}

We can construct a memoryful randomized witness for this property as follows for any
\epsilon\geq 0
.
Let
\sigma_{\alpha}
and
\sigma_{\beta}
be the memoryless deterministic schedulers that choose
\alpha
at
s
and
\beta
at
s
, respectively, and let
\sigma:=[\sigma_{\alpha}\oplus_{0.5}\sigma_{\beta}]
be the convex combination of these two schedulers.
Intuitively,
\sigma
initially throws a fair coin to decide whether to always take
\alpha
or always take
\beta
.
Hence, this scheduler is randomized with one bit of memory.
Using
[
51
, Lem. 3.8]
, we have

If we do not allow memory or randomization, however, the property only holds for
\epsilon\geq 1
.
Firstly, let us assume
\sigma\in\Sigma^{\mathcal{M}}
to be memoryless.
We distinguish two cases: If
\sigma(s)(\alpha)=1
, then we almost-surely visit only
t_{1}
infinitely often and never visit
t_{2}
. Otherwise, if
\sigma(s)(\alpha)<1
, the probability of visiting
t_{1}
infinitely often is 0 and the probability of finally taking
\beta
is 1, hence we almost-surely visit
t_{2}
infinitely often.

Now, let us assume that
\sigma\in\Sigma^{\mathcal{M}}
is memoryful deterministic. Then the DTMC induced by
\sigma
on
\mathcal{M}
has a unique infinite path
\pi
.

\sigma
never takes
\beta
, then
\pi
never reaches
t_{2}
, so

Appendix F
Proofs for
\Cref

F.1
Proof of
\Cref
le:reduce-to-moa

See
72

Proof.

Let us fix an order on
\mathscr{C}=\{c_{1},\ldots,c_{|\mathscr{C}|}\}
.
For
j=1,\ldots,l
and
T\in\mathcal{T}^{j}
, let
\mathscr{C}^{j}(T):=\{c\in\mathscr{C}\mid\exists i\in\mathit{ind}^{j}(c).\ T_{i,j}=T\}
.

We lift the reward structure
\textit{R}_{c}^{j}
(
\Cref
def:rewForComb) from
\mathcal{M}_{c}
to
\mathcal{M}_{\mathscr{C}}
and denote it again as
\textit{R}_{c}^{j}
, under abuse of notation.

We show the equivalence step by step.

(2) Claim:
x

\displaystyle\exists\sigma_{c_{1}}\in\Sigma^{\mathcal{M}_{c_{1}}}\ldots\sigma_{c_{|\mathscr{C}|}}\in\Sigma^{\mathcal{M}_{c_{|\mathscr{C}|}}}.

\displaystyle\bigwedge_{j=1}^{l}\sum_{c\in\mathscr{C}^{j}}\sum_{i\in\mathit{ind}^{j}(c)}q_{i,j}{\text{Pr}}_{s_{c}}^{\mathcal{M}_{c},\sigma_{c}}(\operatorname{\hbox to8.7pt{\vbox to8.7pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 0.25833pt\lower-1.76527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@closescope \hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lxSVG@roundjoin\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-linejoin} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 3.57 L 5.66 9.23 L 11.32 3.57 L 5.66 -2.09 Z}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope }
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}T_{i,j})\bowtie_{j}q_{j}

\displaystyle\mathbin{\textit{ iff }}\quad

\displaystyle\exists\sigma_{c_{1}}\in\Sigma^{\mathcal{M}_{c_{1}}}\ldots\sigma_{c_{|\mathscr{C}|}}\in\Sigma^{\mathcal{M}_{c_{|\mathscr{C}|}}}.

\displaystyle\bigwedge_{j=1}^{l}\sum_{c\in\mathscr{C}^{j}}\sum_{i\in\mathit{ind}^{j}(c)}q_{i,j}\mathbb{E}_{s_{c}}^{\mathcal{M}_{c},\sigma_{c}}(\textit{R}_{T_{i,j}})\bowtie_{j}q_{j}

Proof:
Straightforward.

\proofsubparagraph

(3) Claim:
x

\displaystyle\exists\sigma_{c_{1}}\in\Sigma^{\mathcal{M}_{c_{1}}}\ldots\sigma_{c_{|\mathscr{C}|}}\in\Sigma^{\mathcal{M}_{c_{|\mathscr{C}|}}}.

\displaystyle\bigwedge_{j=1}^{l}\sum_{c\in\mathscr{C}^{j}}\sum_{i\in\mathit{ind}^{j}(c)}q_{i,j}\mathbb{E}_{s_{c}}^{\mathcal{M}_{c},\sigma_{c}}(\textit{R}_{T_{i,j}})\bowtie_{j}q_{j}

\displaystyle\mathbin{\textit{ iff }}\quad

\displaystyle\exists\sigma_{c_{1}}\in\Sigma^{\mathcal{M}_{c_{1}}}\ldots\sigma_{c_{|\mathscr{C}|}}\in\Sigma^{\mathcal{M}_{c_{|\mathscr{C}|}}}.

\displaystyle\bigwedge_{j=1}^{l}\sum_{c\in\mathscr{C}^{j}}\mathbb{E}_{s_{c}}^{\mathcal{M}_{c},\sigma_{c}}\left(\textit{R}_{c}^{j}\right)\bowtie_{j}q_{j}

Proof:

Let
j\in\{1,\ldots,l\}
,
c\in\mathscr{C}
, and
\sigma_{c}\in\Sigma^{\mathcal{M}_{c}}
.
Then,

\displaystyle\sum_{i\in\mathit{ind}^{j}(c)}q_{i,j}\mathbb{E}_{s_{c}}^{\mathcal{M}_{c},\sigma_{c}}(\textit{R}_{T_{i,j}})=

\displaystyle\sum_{T\in\mathcal{T}_{c}\cap\mathcal{T}^{j}}\left(\sum_{i\in\{\mathit{ind}^{j}(c)\mid T=T_{i,j}\}}q_{i,j}\right)\cdot\mathbb{E}_{s_{c}}^{\mathcal{M}_{c},\sigma_{c}}(\textit{R}_{T}(s,\mathcal{T}))

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{s_{c}}^{\mathcal{M}_{c},\sigma_{c}}\left(\sum_{T\in\mathcal{T}_{c}\cap\mathcal{T}^{j}}\left(\sum_{i\in\{\mathit{ind}^{j}(c)\mid T=T_{i,j}\}}q_{i,j}\right)\cdot\textit{R}_{T}(s,\mathcal{T})\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{s_{c}}^{\mathcal{M}_{c},\sigma_{c}}\left(\textit{R}_{c}^{j}\right)~.

\proofsubparagraph

(4) Claim:
x

\displaystyle\exists\sigma_{c_{1}}\in\Sigma^{\mathcal{M}_{c_{1}}}\ldots\sigma_{c_{|\mathscr{C}|}}\in\Sigma^{\mathcal{M}_{c_{|\mathscr{C}|}}}.

\displaystyle\bigwedge_{j=1}^{l}\sum_{c\in\mathscr{C}^{j}}\mathbb{E}_{s_{c}}^{\mathcal{M}_{c},\sigma_{c}}\left(\textit{R}_{c}^{j}\right)\bowtie_{j}q_{j}

\displaystyle\mathbin{\textit{ iff }}\quad

\displaystyle\exists\sigma\in\Sigma^{\mathcal{M}_{\mathscr{C}}}.

\displaystyle\bigwedge_{j=1}^{l}\mathbb{E}_{s_{\mathscr{C}}}^{\mathcal{M}_{\mathscr{C}},\sigma}(\textit{R}^{j})\bowtie_{j}q_{j}

Proof:

Intuitively,
\mathcal{M}_{\mathscr{C}}
corresponds to initial state
s_{\mathscr{C}}
leading with equal probability to one of the goal unfoldings of
\mathcal{M}
with respect to some state-scheduler-combination. Therefore, a number of schedulers for each such goal unfolding can directly be combined into a single scheduler for
\mathcal{M}_{\mathscr{C}}
and vice versa.

Firstly, for
\sigma\in\Sigma^{\mathcal{M}_{\mathscr{C}}}
, let us use
\sigma(s_{\mathscr{C}})
to denote the residual scheduler of
\sigma
after having visited
s_{\mathscr{C}}
,
and observe that for
j\in\{1,\ldots,l\}
we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{s_{\mathscr{C}}}^{\mathcal{M}_{\mathscr{C}},\sigma}(\textit{R}^{j})

\displaystyle=\sum_{c\in\mathscr{C}^{j}}\frac{1}{|\mathscr{C}|}\cdot\mathbb{E}_{(s_{c},c)}^{\mathcal{M}_{\mathscr{C}},\sigma(s_{\mathscr{C}})}(\textit{R}^{j})

\displaystyle=\sum_{c\in\mathscr{C}^{j}}\frac{1}{|\mathscr{C}|}\cdot|\mathscr{C}|\cdot\mathbb{E}_{(s_{c},c)}^{\mathcal{M}_{\mathscr{C}},\sigma(s_{\mathscr{C}})}(\textit{R}_{c}^{j})=\sum_{c\in\mathscr{C}^{j}}\mathbb{E}_{(s_{c},c)}^{\mathcal{M}_{\mathscr{C}},\sigma(s_{\mathscr{C}})}(\textit{R}_{c}^{j})~.

Let us now show both directions of the claim separately.

‘
\Rightarrow
’:
Let
\sigma_{c_{1}}\in\Sigma^{\mathcal{M}_{c_{1}}},\ldots,\sigma_{c_{|\mathscr{C}|}}\in\Sigma^{\mathcal{M}_{c_{|\mathscr{C}|}}}
.
Construct
\sigma\in\Sigma^{\mathcal{M}_{\mathscr{C}}}
as follows:

•

\sigma(s_{\mathscr{C}})(\epsilon)=1
(there is no other action available)

•

All finite paths in
\mathcal{M}_{\mathscr{C}}
from
s_{\mathscr{C}}
are of the form

s_{\mathscr{C}}\epsilon(s_{c},c)\alpha_{1}(s_{1},c)\ldots\alpha_{r}(s_{r},c)

for some
c\in\mathscr{C}
,
s_{1},\ldots,s_{r}\in S_{c}
,
\alpha_{1},\ldots,\alpha_{r}\in\textit{Act}
and
r\in\mathbb{N}
.
Let
h\in\{1,\ldots,n\}
be the unique index such that
c=(s,\sigma_{h})
for some
s\in S
.
We let

\displaystyle\sigma(s_{\mathscr{C}}\epsilon(s_{c},c)\alpha_{1}(s_{1},c)\ldots\alpha_{r}(s_{r},c))

\displaystyle=\sigma_{h}(s_{c}\alpha_{1}s_{1}\ldots\alpha_{r}s_{r})~.

For paths not starting in
s_{\mathscr{C}}
, we pick an action uniformly at random.

Then for
j=1,\ldots,l
we have
\mathbb{E}_{s_{c}}^{\mathcal{M}_{c},\sigma_{c}}(\textit{R}_{c}^{j})=\mathbb{E}_{(s_{c},c)}^{\mathcal{M}_{\mathscr{C}},\sigma(s_{\mathscr{C}})}(\textit{R}_{c}^{j})
by definition of the reward structures and our initial observation.

‘
\Leftarrow
’:
Let
\sigma\in\Sigma^{\mathcal{M}_{\mathscr{C}}}
.
Let
c\in\mathscr{C}
. We construct
\sigma_{c}\in\Sigma^{\mathcal{M}_{c}}
as follows.
For
s_{c}\alpha_{1}s_{1}\ldots\alpha_{r}s_{r}\in\textit{Paths}_{\textit{fin}}^{\mathcal{M}_{c}}(s_{c})
we let

\sigma_{c}(s_{c}\alpha_{1}s_{1}\ldots\alpha_{r}s_{r})=\sigma(s_{\mathscr{C}}\epsilon(s_{c},c)\alpha_{1}(s_{1},c)\ldots\alpha_{r}(s_{r},c))

and for all other paths we choose some action uniformly at random.

Then for
j=1,\ldots,l
we have
\mathbb{E}_{s_{c}}^{\mathcal{M}_{c},\sigma_{c}}(\textit{R}_{c}^{j})=\mathbb{E}_{(s_{c},c)}^{\mathcal{M}_{\mathscr{C}},\sigma(s_{\mathscr{C}})}(\textit{R}_{c}^{j})
by definition of the reward structures and our initial observation.
∎

Appendix G
Proofs for
\Cref
sec:moa

G.1
Proof of
\Cref
th:moa_preproc_transfer-MR

See
78

We show this claim in two steps, analogously to
[
24
]
.
First, we show that any scheduler for
\mathcal{M}_{\mathscr{C}}
induces a scheduler for
\overline{\mathcal{M}_{\mathscr{C}}}
that reaches
S_{\bot}
with probability 1, and vice versa in
\Cref
le:moa_preproc_transfer-memory.
Then we show that memoryless (randomized) schedulers suffice on
\overline{\mathcal{M}_{\mathscr{C}}}
in
\Cref
le:moa_preproc_MR-suffice.

Lemma 93
.

\displaystyle\exists\sigma\in\Sigma^{\mathcal{M}_{\mathscr{C}}}.\ \bigwedge_{j=1}^{l}\mathbb{E}_{s_{\mathscr{C}}}^{\mathcal{M}_{\mathscr{C}},\sigma}(\textit{R}^{j})\bowtie_{j}q_{j}

\displaystyle\mathbin{\textit{ iff }}\quad

\displaystyle\exists\overline{\sigma}\in\Sigma^{\overline{\mathcal{M}_{\mathscr{C}}}}.\ \bigwedge_{j=1}^{l}\mathbb{E}_{s_{\mathscr{C}}}^{{\overline{\mathcal{M}_{\mathscr{C}}}},\overline{\sigma}}(\textit{R}^{j})\bowtie_{j}q_{j}\wedge{\text{Pr}}_{s_{\mathscr{C}}}^{{\overline{\mathcal{M}_{\mathscr{C}}}},\overline{\sigma}}(\operatorname{\hbox to8.7pt{\vbox to8.7pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 0.25833pt\lower-1.76527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@closescope \hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lxSVG@roundjoin\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-linejoin} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 3.57 L 5.66 9.23 L 11.32 3.57 L 5.66 -2.09 Z}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope }
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}S_{\bot})=1

Proof (Sketch).

“
\Rightarrow
”:
Let
\sigma\in\Sigma^{\mathcal{M}_{\mathscr{C}}}
.
We proceed similarly to the scheduler witness transfer from, e.g.,
[
39
, Th. 1]
.
For every MEC
C=(S^{\prime},A)
and finite path
\pi\in\textit{Paths}_{\textit{fin}}^{\mathcal{M}_{\mathscr{C}}}(s_{\mathscr{C}})
that has just entered
C
(i.e.,
\pi=\pi^{\prime}\alpha s
and
s\in S^{\prime}
and
\alpha\not\in A
), we compute the probability
p_{C,\pi}
of staying in
C
forever after having taken
\pi
.
We then construct
\overline{\sigma}\in\Sigma^{\overline{\mathcal{M}_{\mathscr{C}}}}
from
\sigma\in\Sigma^{\mathcal{M}_{\mathscr{C}}}
as follows for
\pi\in\textit{Paths}_{\textit{fin}}^{\overline{\mathcal{M}_{\mathscr{C}}}}(s_{\mathscr{C}})
with
\textit{last}(\pi)\in S_{\mathscr{C}}
:

•

If
\pi
ends in a state that is not contained in an MEC: Behave like
\sigma
, i.e.
\overline{\sigma}(\pi)=\sigma(\pi)
(observe that
\pi
can be interpreted as a path in
\mathcal{M}_{\mathscr{C}}
since it does not enter
S_{\bot}
by assumption)

•

If
\pi
just entered some MEC
C
:
If
p_{C,\pi}=1
then we deterministically take
\dagger
, otherwise we copy
\sigma
.

Observe that the set of paths in
\mathcal{M}_{\mathscr{C}}^{\sigma}
which from some point on almost-surely stay in an EC has measure 1.
Hence, the set of paths in
{\overline{\mathcal{M}_{\mathscr{C}}}}^{\overline{\sigma}}
which at some point take
\dagger
to transition to
S_{\bot}
also has measure 1.

“
\Leftarrow
”:
Given
\overline{\sigma}\in\Sigma^{\overline{\mathcal{M}_{\mathscr{C}}}}
, construct
\sigma\in\Sigma^{\mathcal{M}_{\mathscr{C}}}
by behaving like
\overline{\sigma}
unless
\overline{\sigma}
chooses
\dagger
, then the current state must be contained in some MEC
C
and there must exists a memoryless deterministic scheduler
\sigma_{C}
staying inside
C
forever almost-surely, so
\sigma
switches to behave like
\sigma_{C}
instead. Formally, for
\pi\in\textit{Paths}_{\textit{fin}}^{\mathcal{M}_{\mathscr{C}}}(s_{\mathscr{C}})
and
\alpha\in\textit{Act}(\textit{last}(\pi))
:

\sigma(\pi)(\alpha)=\begin{cases}\overline{\sigma}(\pi)(\alpha)+\overline{\sigma}(\pi)(\dagger)\cdot\sigma_{C}(\textit{last}(\pi))(\alpha)&\text{if }\exists C=(S^{\prime},A)\in\textit{MEC}(\mathcal{M}_{\mathscr{C}}).\ \textit{last}(\pi)\in S^{\prime}\\
\overline{\sigma}(\pi)(\alpha)&\text{otherwise}~.\end{cases}

∎

Before showing
\Cref
le:moa_preproc_MR-suffice, let us first recall the definition of expected visiting times.

Definition 94

(Expected visiting times)
.

Given some MDP
\mathcal{M}=(S,\allowbreak\textit{Act},\allowbreak\mathbf{P})
with some initial state
s_{0}\in S
and some scheduler
\sigma\in\Sigma^{\mathcal{M}}
, for
s\in S
and
\alpha\in\textit{Act}(s)
we let

\displaystyle\mathit{vis}^{\mathcal{M},\sigma,s_{0}}(s,\alpha)

\displaystyle=\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}\left(\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\pi\in\textit{Paths}_{\textit{fin}}^{\mathcal{M}}(s_{0}),|\pi|=j+1\\
\exists\pi^{\prime}\in\textit{Paths}_{\textit{fin}}^{\mathcal{M}}(s_{0}),s^{\prime}\in S.\ \pi=\pi^{\prime}s\alpha s^{\prime}\end{subarray}}{\text{Pr}}^{\mathcal{M},\sigma}_{s_{0}}(\pi)\right)

\displaystyle\mathit{vis}^{\mathcal{M},\sigma,s_{0}}(s)

\displaystyle=\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}\left(\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\pi\in\textit{Paths}_{\textit{fin}}^{\mathcal{M}},|\pi|=j\\
\textit{last}(\pi)=s\end{subarray}}{\text{Pr}}^{\mathcal{M},\sigma}_{s_{0}}(\pi)\right)

and further

\displaystyle\mathit{InfA}^{\sigma}

\displaystyle=\{(s,\alpha)\in S\times\textit{Act}\mid\mathit{vis}^{\sigma}(s,\alpha)=\infty\}

\displaystyle\mathit{FinA}^{\sigma}

\displaystyle=(S\times\textit{Act})\setminus\mathit{InfA}^{\sigma}

\displaystyle\mathit{InfS}^{\sigma}

\displaystyle=\{s\in S\mid\mathit{vis}^{\sigma}(s)=\infty\}

\displaystyle\mathit{FinS}^{\sigma}

\displaystyle=S\setminus\mathit{InfS}^{\sigma}~.

We often omit the MDP and/or initial state if clear from context.
Observe that
(\mathit{InfS}^{\sigma},\mathit{InfA}^{\sigma})
must be contained in MECs of
\mathcal{M}
.
For
s\in\mathit{FinS}^{\sigma}
we have
\mathit{vis}^{\sigma}(s)=\sum_{\alpha\in\textit{Act}(s)}\mathit{vis}^{\sigma}(\alpha)
.

implies

\displaystyle\exists\overline{\sigma}^{\prime}\in\Sigma^{\overline{\mathcal{M}_{\mathscr{C}}}}_{\textit{MR}}.\ \bigwedge_{j=1}^{l}\mathbb{E}_{s_{\mathscr{C}}}^{{\overline{\mathcal{M}_{\mathscr{C}}}},\overline{\sigma}^{\prime}}(\textit{R}^{j})=x_{j}\wedge{\text{Pr}}_{s_{\mathscr{C}}}^{{\overline{\mathcal{M}_{\mathscr{C}}}},\overline{\sigma}^{\prime}}(\operatorname{\hbox to8.7pt{\vbox to8.7pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 0.25833pt\lower-1.76527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@closescope \hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lxSVG@roundjoin\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-linejoin} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 3.57 L 5.66 9.23 L 11.32 3.57 L 5.66 -2.09 Z}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces

\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}S_{\bot})=1~.

We show this claim analogously to
[
24
, Prop. 6]
.
Let
x_{1},\ldots,x_{l}\in\mathbb{R}
.
Let
\overline{\sigma}\in\Sigma^{\overline{\mathcal{M}_{\mathscr{C}}}}
with
\mathbb{E}_{s_{\mathscr{C}}}^{{\overline{\mathcal{M}_{\mathscr{C}}}},\overline{\sigma}}(\textit{R}^{j})=x_{j}\wedge{\text{Pr}}_{s_{\mathscr{C}}}^{{\overline{\mathcal{M}_{\mathscr{C}}}},\overline{\sigma}}(\operatorname{\hbox to8.7pt{\vbox to8.7pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 0.25833pt\lower-1.76527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@closescope \hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lxSVG@roundjoin\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-linejoin} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 3.57 L 5.66 9.23 L 11.32 3.57 L 5.66 -2.09 Z}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope }
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}S_{\bot})=1
.
Since
{\text{Pr}}_{s_{\mathscr{C}}}^{{\overline{\mathcal{M}_{\mathscr{C}}}},\overline{\sigma}}(\operatorname{\hbox to8.7pt{\vbox to8.7pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 0.25833pt\lower-1.76527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@closescope \hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lxSVG@roundjoin\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-linejoin} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 3.57 L 5.66 9.23 L 11.32 3.57 L 5.66 -2.09 Z}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope }
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}S_{\bot})=1
, we have
\mathit{InfS}^{\overline{\sigma}}\subseteq S_{\bot}
, which further implies
\mathit{InfA}^{\overline{\sigma}}=\{(s,\alpha)\mid s\in\mathit{InfS}^{\overline{\sigma}},\alpha\in\overline{\textit{Act}_{\mathscr{C}}}(\mathit{InfS}^{\overline{\sigma}})\}
.

We construct a memoryless witness for the original claim as follows for
s\in\overline{S_{\mathscr{C}}}
,
\alpha\in\overline{\textit{Act}_{\mathscr{C}}}(s)
:

\overline{\sigma}^{\prime}(s,\alpha)=\begin{cases}\frac{\mathit{vis}^{\overline{\sigma}}(s,\alpha)}{\sum_{\alpha^{\prime}\in\overline{\textit{Act}_{\mathscr{C}}}(s)}\mathit{vis}^{\overline{\sigma}}(s,\alpha^{\prime})}&\text{if }s\not\in\mathit{InfS}^{\overline{\sigma}}\\
1&\text{else (for }s\in\mathit{InfS}^{\overline{\sigma}}\text{ we have }\{\alpha\}=\overline{\textit{Act}_{\mathscr{C}}}(s)\text{)}~.\end{cases}

In order to see that
\overline{\sigma}^{\prime}
satisfies the desired properties, we first show that the expected visiting times of actions that are visited finitely often under
\overline{\sigma}
match.

\proofsubparagraph

Claim 1:

For all
(s,\alpha)\in\mathit{FinA}^{\overline{\sigma}}\cap\mathit{FinA}^{\overline{\sigma}^{\prime}}
we have

\mathit{vis}^{\overline{\sigma}}(s,\alpha)=\mathit{vis}^{\overline{\sigma}^{\prime}}(s,\alpha)
.

For
(s,\alpha)\in\mathit{FinA}^{\overline{\sigma}}\cap\mathit{FinA}^{\overline{\sigma}^{\prime}}
we let
d_{(s,\alpha)}=\frac{\mathit{vis}^{\overline{\sigma}}(s,\alpha)}{\mathit{vis}^{\overline{\sigma}^{\prime}}(s,\alpha)}
.

Towards contradiction, assume there exists
(s,\alpha)\in\mathit{FinA}^{\overline{\sigma}}\cap\mathit{FinA}^{\overline{\sigma}^{\prime}}
with
d_{(s,\alpha)}\neq 1
.
We assume
\max_{(s,\alpha)\in\mathit{FinA}^{\overline{\sigma}}\cap\mathit{FinA}^{\overline{\sigma}^{\prime}}}d_{(s,\alpha)}>1
and set
d^{*}:=\max_{(s,\alpha)\in\mathit{FinA}^{\overline{\sigma}}\cap\mathit{FinA}^{\overline{\sigma}^{\prime}}}d_{(s,\alpha)}
and
(s^{*},\alpha^{*})=\argmax_{(s,\alpha)\in\mathit{FinA}^{\overline{\sigma}}\cap\mathit{FinA}^{\overline{\sigma}^{\prime}}}d_{(s,\alpha)}
.
If the maximum is smaller than 1, then take the minimal value and argue analogously.

Let
\pi\in\textit{Paths}_{\textit{fin}}^{\overline{\mathcal{M}_{\mathscr{C}}}}(s_{\mathscr{C}})
with
\pi=\pi^{\prime}s^{*}\alpha^{*}s^{\prime}
for some
\pi^{\prime}\in\textit{Paths}_{\textit{fin}}^{\overline{\mathcal{M}_{\mathscr{C}}}}(s_{\mathscr{C}})
,
s^{\prime}\in\overline{S_{\mathscr{C}}}
.
Observe that
s^{*}\not\in\mathit{InfS}^{\overline{\sigma}}
since
\mathit{InfS}^{\overline{\sigma}}\subseteq S_{\bot}
and thus all outgoing actions of states in
\mathit{InfS}^{\overline{\sigma}}
must also be taken infinitely often, but
(s^{*},\alpha^{*})\in\mathit{FinA}^{\overline{\sigma}}
by assumption.

By definition,

\frac{\mathit{vis}^{\overline{\sigma}}(s^{*},\alpha^{*})}{\sum_{\alpha^{\prime}\in\overline{\textit{Act}_{\mathscr{C}}}(s^{*})}\mathit{vis}^{\overline{\sigma}}(s^{*},\alpha^{\prime})}=\overline{\sigma}^{\prime}(s^{*})(\alpha^{*})

and
\mathit{vis}^{\overline{\sigma}}(s^{*})=\sum_{\alpha^{\prime}\in\overline{\textit{Act}_{\mathscr{C}}}(s^{*})}\mathit{vis}^{\overline{\sigma}}(s^{*},\alpha^{\prime})
.
Since
\overline{\sigma}^{\prime}
is memoryless we have
\mathit{vis}^{\overline{\sigma}^{\prime}}(s^{*},\alpha^{*})=\mathit{vis}^{\overline{\sigma}^{\prime}}(s^{*})\cdot\overline{\sigma}^{\prime}(s^{*},\alpha^{*})
.
Putting everything together, we have
\mathit{vis}^{\overline{\sigma}}(s^{*})=d^{*}\cdot\mathit{vis}^{\overline{\sigma}^{\prime}}(s^{*})
.

If
s^{*}\neq s_{\mathscr{C}}
, then

\displaystyle\sum_{s^{\prime}\in\overline{S_{\mathscr{C}}}}\sum_{\alpha^{\prime}\in\overline{\textit{Act}_{\mathscr{C}}}(s^{\prime})}\mathit{vis}^{\overline{\sigma}^{\prime}}(s^{\prime},\alpha^{\prime})\cdot\mathbf{P}(s^{\prime},\alpha^{\prime},s^{*})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathit{vis}^{\overline{\sigma}^{\prime}}(s^{*})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{d^{*}}\cdot\mathit{vis}^{\overline{\sigma}}(s^{*})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{d^{*}}\cdot\sum_{s^{\prime}\in\overline{S_{\mathscr{C}}}}\sum_{\alpha^{\prime}\in\overline{\textit{Act}_{\mathscr{C}}}(s^{\prime})}\mathit{vis}^{\overline{\sigma}}(s^{\prime},\alpha^{\prime})\cdot\mathbf{P}(s^{\prime},\alpha^{\prime},s^{*})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{d^{*}}\cdot\sum_{s^{\prime}\in\overline{S_{\mathscr{C}}}}\sum_{\alpha^{\prime}\in\overline{\textit{Act}_{\mathscr{C}}}(s^{\prime})}d_{\alpha^{\prime}}\cdot\mathit{vis}^{\overline{\sigma}^{\prime}}(s^{\prime},\alpha^{\prime})\cdot\mathbf{P}(s^{\prime},\alpha^{\prime},s^{*})

which implies that
d_{\alpha^{\prime}}=d^{*}
for all
\alpha^{\prime}
leading to
s^{*}
with positive probability.
In particular, for the second-to-last action
\alpha_{|\pi|-1}
in
\pi
we must also have
d_{\alpha_{|\pi|-1}}=d^{*}
, i.e., this action must also be
\alpha^{*}
. By repeatedly applying this reasoning we arrive at
s^{*}=s_{\mathscr{C}}
, contradicting our assumption.

So let us assume
s^{*}=s_{\mathscr{C}}
, then

\displaystyle 1+\sum_{s^{\prime}\in\overline{S_{\mathscr{C}}}}\sum_{\alpha^{\prime}\in\overline{\textit{Act}_{\mathscr{C}}}(s^{\prime})}\mathit{vis}^{\overline{\sigma}^{\prime}}(s^{\prime},\alpha^{\prime})\cdot\overline{\mathbf{P}_{\mathscr{C}}}(s^{\prime},\alpha^{\prime},s^{*})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathit{vis}^{\overline{\sigma}^{\prime}}(s^{*})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{d^{*}}\mathit{vis}^{\overline{\sigma}}(s^{*})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{d^{*}}\cdot\left(1+\sum_{s^{\prime}\in\overline{S_{\mathscr{C}}}}\sum_{\alpha^{\prime}\in\overline{\textit{Act}_{\mathscr{C}}}(s^{\prime})}\mathit{vis}^{\overline{\sigma}}(s^{\prime},\alpha^{\prime})\cdot\overline{\mathbf{P}_{\mathscr{C}}}(s^{\prime},\alpha^{\prime},s^{*})\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{d^{*}}\cdot\left(1+\sum_{s^{\prime}\in\overline{S_{\mathscr{C}}}}\sum_{\alpha^{\prime}\in\overline{\textit{Act}_{\mathscr{C}}}(s^{\prime})}d_{\alpha^{\prime}}\cdot\mathit{vis}^{\overline{\sigma}^{\prime}}(s^{\prime},\alpha^{\prime})\cdot\overline{\mathbf{P}_{\mathscr{C}}}(s^{\prime},\alpha^{\prime},s^{*})\right)

but the equality between the first and last term would imply that some
d_{\alpha^{\prime}}>d^{*}
, which contradicts our assumption that
d^{*}
is maximal.

\proofsubparagraph

Claim 2:
\overline{\sigma}^{\prime}
satisfies the desired properties.

Recall that
\mathit{InfS}^{\overline{\sigma}}
and
\mathit{InfS}^{\overline{\sigma}^{\prime}}
must be contained in MECs of
\overline{\mathcal{M}_{\mathscr{C}}}
and recall also that the reward functions
\textit{R}^{j}
do not collect reward inside MECs by construction.
23
23

23

It would suffice here to reason that the expected reward is finite for any reward structure
\textit{R}^{j}
; thus this reasoning generalizes to any MDP
\mathcal{M}
with reward functions
\textit{R}^{j}
whose expected reward is finite under all schedulers.

Therefore, we have
\textit{R}^{j}(s)=0
for
s\in\mathit{InfS}^{\overline{\sigma}}\cup\mathit{InfS}^{\overline{\sigma}^{\prime}}
.
Further, for
s\in\mathit{FinS}^{\overline{\sigma}}
we have
\{s\}\times\overline{\textit{Act}_{\mathscr{C}}}(s)\subseteq\mathit{FinA}^{\overline{\sigma}}
and analogously for
s\in\mathit{FinS}^{\overline{\sigma}^{\prime}}
we have
\{s\}\times\overline{\textit{Act}_{\mathscr{C}}}(s)\subseteq\mathit{FinA}^{\overline{\sigma}^{\prime}}

and thus for
s\in\mathit{FinS}^{\overline{\sigma}}\cap\mathit{FinS}^{\overline{\sigma}^{\prime}}
we have
\{s\}\times\overline{\textit{Act}_{\mathscr{C}}}(s)\subseteq\mathit{FinA}^{\overline{\sigma}}\cap\mathit{FinA}^{\overline{\sigma}^{\prime}}
.

Hence,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{s_{\mathscr{C}}}^{{\overline{\mathcal{M}_{\mathscr{C}}}},\overline{\sigma}^{\prime}}(\textit{R}^{j})

\displaystyle{}=

\displaystyle\sum_{s\in\overline{S_{\mathscr{C}}}}\mathit{vis}^{\overline{\sigma}^{\prime}}(s)\cdot\textit{R}^{j}(s)

\displaystyle{}=

\displaystyle\sum_{s\in\mathit{FinS}^{\overline{\sigma}}\cap\mathit{FinS}^{\overline{\sigma}^{\prime}}}\sum_{\alpha\in\overline{\textit{Act}_{\mathscr{C}}}(s)}\mathit{vis}^{\overline{\sigma}^{\prime}}(s,\alpha)\cdot\textit{R}^{j}(s)

\displaystyle\overset{\text{Cl.~2}}{=}\!\!

\displaystyle\sum_{s\in\mathit{FinS}^{\overline{\sigma}}\cap\mathit{FinS}^{\overline{\sigma}^{\prime}}}\sum_{\alpha\in\overline{\textit{Act}_{\mathscr{C}}}(s)}\mathit{vis}^{\overline{\sigma}}(s,\alpha)\cdot\textit{R}^{j}(s)

\displaystyle{}=

\displaystyle\sum_{s\in\overline{S_{\mathscr{C}}}}\mathit{vis}^{\overline{\sigma}}(s)\cdot\textit{R}^{j}(s)=\mathbb{E}_{s_{\mathscr{C}}}^{{\overline{\mathcal{M}_{\mathscr{C}}}},\overline{\sigma}}(\textit{R}^{j})=x_{j}.

Further, let
\textit{In}(S_{\bot})=\{(s,\alpha)\in\overline{S_{\mathscr{C}}}\times\overline{\textit{Act}_{\mathscr{C}}}\mid\exists t\in S_{\bot}.\ \overline{\mathbf{P}_{\mathscr{C}}}(s,\alpha,t)=1\}
.
Observe that, by construction actions leading to
S_{\bot}
can be visited at most once in expectation and thus
\textit{In}(S_{\bot})\subseteq\mathit{FinA}^{\overline{\sigma}}\cap\mathit{FinA}^{\overline{\sigma}^{\prime}}
.
Then,

\displaystyle{\text{Pr}}_{s_{\mathscr{C}}}^{{\overline{\mathcal{M}_{\mathscr{C}}}},\overline{\sigma}^{\prime}}(\operatorname{\hbox to8.7pt{\vbox to8.7pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 0.25833pt\lower-1.76527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@closescope \hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lxSVG@roundjoin\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-linejoin} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 3.57 L 5.66 9.23 L 11.32 3.57 L 5.66 -2.09 Z}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces

\displaystyle=\sum_{(s,\alpha)\in\textit{In}(S_{\bot})}\mathit{vis}^{\overline{\sigma}^{\prime}}(s,\alpha)\overset{\text{Cl.\ 2}}{=}\sum_{\alpha\in\textit{In}(S_{\bot})}\mathit{vis}^{\overline{\sigma}}(\alpha)={\text{Pr}}_{s_{\mathscr{C}}}^{{\overline{\mathcal{M}_{\mathscr{C}}}},\overline{\sigma}}(\operatorname{\hbox to8.7pt{\vbox to8.7pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 0.25833pt\lower-1.76527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@closescope \hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {

G.2
Proof of

Proof.

It suffices to show that for

, it holds that:

\displaystyle\exists\overline{\sigma}\in\Sigma^{\overline{\mathcal{M}_{\mathscr{C}}}}_{\textit{MR}}.\ \bigwedge_{j=1}^{l}\mathbb{E}_{s_{\mathscr{C}}}^{{\overline{\mathcal{M}_{\mathscr{C}}}},\sigma}(\textit{R}^{j})=x_{j}\wedge{\text{Pr}}_{s_{\mathscr{C}}}^{{\overline{\mathcal{M}_{\mathscr{C}}}},\sigma}(\operatorname{\hbox to8.7pt{\vbox to8.7pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 0.25833pt\lower-1.76527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@closescope \hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {

\displaystyle\mathbin{\textit{ iff }}\quad

.

We proceed analogously to
[
22
, Th. 3.2 (1.)
\mathbin{\textit{ iff }}

.
We show both directions separately; let us first introduce some notation used in both directions.
Given some
\overline{\sigma}\in\Sigma^{\overline{\mathcal{M}_{\mathscr{C}}}}_{\textit{MR}}
, we let
\mathbf{P}_{\mathscr{C}}^{\overline{\sigma}}
(under abuse of notation) denote the transition matrix of
\overline{\mathcal{M}_{\mathscr{C}}}
restricted to
S_{\mathscr{C}}
, i.e., the matrix defined by
(\mathbf{P}_{\mathscr{C}}^{\overline{\sigma}})_{s,s^{\prime}}=\sum_{\alpha\in\textit{Act}_{\mathscr{C}}(s)}\mathbf{P}_{\mathscr{C}}(s,\alpha,s^{\prime})\cdot\overline{\sigma}(s,\alpha)
for
s,s^{\prime}\in S_{\mathscr{C}}
.
For
n\in\mathbb{N}
,
((\mathbf{P}_{\mathscr{C}}^{\overline{\sigma}})^{n})_{s,s^{\prime}}
is the probability of transitioning from
s
to
s^{\prime}
in
n
steps.

\proofsubparagraph

“
\Rightarrow
”:
Let
\overline{\sigma}\in\Sigma^{\overline{\mathcal{M}_{\mathscr{C}}}}_{\textit{MR}}
such that

\bigwedge_{j=1}^{l}\mathbb{E}_{s_{\mathscr{C}}}^{{\overline{\mathcal{M}_{\mathscr{C}}}},\overline{\sigma}}(\textit{R}^{j})=x_{j}\wedge{\text{Pr}}_{s_{\mathscr{C}}}^{{\overline{\mathcal{M}_{\mathscr{C}}}},\overline{\sigma}}(\operatorname{\hbox to8.7pt{\vbox to8.7pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 0.25833pt\lower-1.76527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@closescope \hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lxSVG@roundjoin\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-linejoin} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 3.57 L 5.66 9.23 L 11.32 3.57 L 5.66 -2.09 Z}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope }
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}S_{\bot})=1~.

For
s\in S_{\mathscr{C}}
and
\alpha\in\overline{\textit{Act}_{\mathscr{C}}}(s)
, we let

\displaystyle y^{\prime}_{s,\alpha}

\displaystyle=\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}((\mathbf{P}_{\mathscr{C}}^{\overline{\sigma}})^{n})_{s_{\mathscr{C}},s}\cdot\overline{\sigma}(s,\alpha)

\displaystyle y^{\prime}_{s}

\displaystyle=\sum_{s^{\prime}\in S_{\mathscr{C}}}\sum_{\alpha^{\prime}\in\textit{Act}_{\mathscr{C}}(s^{\prime})}\mathbf{P}_{\mathscr{C}}(s^{\prime},\alpha^{\prime},s)\cdot y^{\prime}_{s^{\prime},\alpha^{\prime}}

Since
{\text{Pr}}_{s_{\mathscr{C}}}^{{\overline{\mathcal{M}_{\mathscr{C}}}},\overline{\sigma}}(\operatorname{\hbox to8.7pt{\vbox to8.7pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 0.25833pt\lower-1.76527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@closescope \hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lxSVG@roundjoin\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-linejoin} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 3.57 L 5.66 9.23 L 11.32 3.57 L 5.66 -2.09 Z}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope }
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}S_{\bot})=1
, and
S_{\bot}
is absorbing, the matrix
\mathbf{P}_{\mathscr{C}}^{\overline{\sigma}}
must be sub-stochastic.
Since
\mathbf{P}_{\mathscr{C}}^{\overline{\sigma}}
is sub-stochastic,
y^{\prime}_{s,\alpha}
must be finite and thus the values
y^{\prime}_{s}
and
y^{\prime}_{s,\alpha}
are well-defined.

Observe that
y^{\prime}_{s,\alpha}
is the expected number of times we leave
s\in S_{\mathscr{C}}
via
\alpha\in\overline{\textit{Act}_{\mathscr{C}}}(s)
when starting from
s_{\mathscr{C}}
in
\overline{\mathcal{M}_{\mathscr{C}}}
under
\overline{\sigma}
.
Consequently,
\sum_{\alpha\in\overline{\textit{Act}_{\mathscr{C}}}(s)}y^{\prime}_{s,\alpha}=\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}((\mathbf{P}_{\mathscr{C}}^{\overline{\sigma}})^{n})_{s_{\mathscr{C}},s}
is the expected number of times we transition out of
s\in S_{\mathscr{C}}
under
\overline{\sigma}
when starting from
s_{\mathscr{C}}
.
Conversely,
y^{\prime}_{s}
is the expected number of times we transition
into

s
, i.e.,
y^{\prime}_{s}=\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}((\mathbf{P}_{\mathscr{C}}^{\overline{\sigma}})^{n})_{s_{\mathscr{C}},s}-[s=s_{\mathscr{C}}]
, with the following reasoning:

\displaystyle y^{\prime}_{s}=

\displaystyle\sum_{s^{\prime}\in S_{\mathscr{C}}}\sum_{\alpha^{\prime}\in\textit{Act}_{\mathscr{C}}(s^{\prime})}\mathbf{P}_{\mathscr{C}}(s^{\prime},\alpha^{\prime},s)\cdot y^{\prime}_{s^{\prime},\alpha^{\prime}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{s^{\prime}\in S_{\mathscr{C}}}\sum_{\alpha^{\prime}\in\textit{Act}_{\mathscr{C}}(s^{\prime})}\mathbf{P}_{\mathscr{C}}(s^{\prime},\alpha^{\prime},s)\cdot\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}((\mathbf{P}_{\mathscr{C}}^{\overline{\sigma}})^{n})_{s_{\mathscr{C}},s^{\prime}}\cdot\overline{\sigma}(s^{\prime},\alpha^{\prime})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{s^{\prime}\in S_{\mathscr{C}}}\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}((\mathbf{P}_{\mathscr{C}}^{\overline{\sigma}})^{n})_{s_{\mathscr{C}},s^{\prime}}\cdot\sum_{\alpha^{\prime}\in\textit{Act}_{\mathscr{C}}(s^{\prime})}\mathbf{P}_{\mathscr{C}}(s^{\prime},\alpha^{\prime},s)\cdot\overline{\sigma}(s^{\prime},\alpha^{\prime})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{s^{\prime}\in S_{\mathscr{C}}}\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}((\mathbf{P}_{\mathscr{C}}^{\overline{\sigma}})^{n})_{s_{\mathscr{C}},s^{\prime}}\cdot(\mathbf{P}_{\mathscr{C}}^{\overline{\sigma}})_{s^{\prime},s}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}((\mathbf{P}_{\mathscr{C}}^{\overline{\sigma}})^{n+1})_{s_{\mathscr{C}},s}=\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}((\mathbf{P}_{\mathscr{C}}^{\overline{\sigma}})^{n})_{s_{\mathscr{C}},s}=\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}((\mathbf{P}_{\mathscr{C}}^{\overline{\sigma}})^{n})_{s_{\mathscr{C}},s}-[s=s_{\mathscr{C}}]~.

Using this, let us now show that
y^{\prime}
is indeed a feasible solution of the encoding.

(
7.3
)

For
s\in S_{\mathscr{C}}
, by the above reasoning we have

\displaystyle\sum_{\alpha\in\overline{\textit{Act}_{\mathscr{C}}}(s)}y^{\prime}_{s,\alpha}-\sum_{s^{\prime}\in S_{\mathscr{C}}}\sum_{\alpha^{\prime}\in\textit{Act}_{\mathscr{C}}(s^{\prime})}\mathbf{P}_{\mathscr{C}}(s^{\prime},\alpha^{\prime},s)\cdot y^{\prime}_{s^{\prime},\alpha^{\prime}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}((\mathbf{P}_{\mathscr{C}}^{\overline{\sigma}})^{n})_{s_{\mathscr{C}},s}-\left(\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}((\mathbf{P}_{\mathscr{C}}^{\overline{\sigma}})^{n})_{s_{\mathscr{C}},s}-[s=s_{\mathscr{C}}]\right)=[s=s_{\mathscr{C}}]~.

(
7.4
)

Let
j=1,\ldots,l
. We show that
\sum_{s\in S_{\mathscr{C}}}\textit{R}^{j}(s)y^{\prime}_{s}=\mathbb{E}_{s_{\mathscr{C}}}^{{\overline{\mathcal{M}_{\mathscr{C}}}},\sigma}(\textit{R}^{j})
.

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{s_{\mathscr{C}}}^{{\overline{\mathcal{M}_{\mathscr{C}}}},\sigma}(\textit{R}^{j})

\displaystyle=\int_{\pi\in\textit{Paths}^{\overline{\mathcal{M}_{\mathscr{C}}}}(s_{\mathscr{C}})}\sum_{i=0}^{\infty}\textit{R}^{j}(\pi(i))\,d{\text{Pr}}^{\overline{\mathcal{M}_{\mathscr{C}}},\overline{\sigma}}_{s_{\mathscr{C}}}

\displaystyle=\sum_{i=0}^{\infty}\int_{\pi\in\textit{Paths}^{\overline{\mathcal{M}_{\mathscr{C}}}}(s_{\mathscr{C}})}\textit{R}^{j}(\pi(i))\,d{\text{Pr}}^{\overline{\mathcal{M}_{\mathscr{C}}},\overline{\sigma}}_{s_{\mathscr{C}}}

\displaystyle=\sum_{i=0}^{\infty}\sum_{\pi\in\textit{Paths}_{\textit{fin}}^{\overline{\mathcal{M}_{\mathscr{C}}}}(s_{\mathscr{C}}),|\pi|=i}{\text{Pr}}^{\overline{\mathcal{M}_{\mathscr{C}}},\overline{\sigma}}_{s_{\mathscr{C}}}(\pi)\cdot\textit{R}^{j}(\pi(i))

Observe that
\textit{R}^{j}(s_{\mathscr{C}})=0
and
\textit{R}^{j}(S_{\bot})=0
and hence we can rewrite the above further to

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{s_{\mathscr{C}}}^{{\overline{\mathcal{M}_{\mathscr{C}}}},\sigma}(\textit{R}^{j})

\displaystyle=\sum_{s\in S_{\mathscr{C}}}\sum_{i=1}^{\infty}\sum_{\pi\in\textit{Paths}_{\textit{fin}}^{\overline{\mathcal{M}_{\mathscr{C}}}}(s_{\mathscr{C}}),|\pi|=i,\textit{last}(\pi)=s}{\text{Pr}}^{\overline{\mathcal{M}_{\mathscr{C}}},\overline{\sigma}}_{s_{\mathscr{C}}}(\pi)\cdot\textit{R}^{j}(\pi(i))

\displaystyle=\sum_{s\in S_{\mathscr{C}}}\textit{R}^{j}(\pi(i))\cdot\sum_{i=1}^{\infty}\sum_{\pi\in\textit{Paths}_{\textit{fin}}^{\overline{\mathcal{M}_{\mathscr{C}}}}(s_{\mathscr{C}}),|\pi|=i,\textit{last}(\pi)=s}{\text{Pr}}^{\overline{\mathcal{M}_{\mathscr{C}}},\overline{\sigma}}_{s_{\mathscr{C}}}(\pi)

\displaystyle=\sum_{s\in S_{\mathscr{C}}}\textit{R}^{j}(\pi(i))\cdot\sum_{i=1}^{\infty}((\mathbf{P}_{\mathscr{C}}^{\overline{\sigma}})^{i})_{s_{\mathscr{C}},s}=\sum_{s\in S_{\mathscr{C}}}\textit{R}^{j}(\pi(i))\cdot y^{\prime}_{s}

(
7.5
)

y^{\prime}_{s,\dagger}
is the expected number of times we leave
s
via
\dagger
and move to

.
Hence,

to

. Since

is absorbing, this corresponds to the expected number of times we move to

for the first time
, i.e.,
{\text{Pr}}_{s_{\mathscr{C}}}^{{\overline{\mathcal{M}_{\mathscr{C}}}},\overline{\sigma}}(\operatorname{\hbox to8.7pt{\vbox to8.7pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 0.25833pt\lower-1.76527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@closescope \hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lxSVG@roundjoin\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-linejoin} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 3.57 L 5.66 9.23 L 11.32 3.57 L 5.66 -2.09 Z}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope }
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}S_{\bot})
.
By assumption it holds that
{\text{Pr}}_{s_{\mathscr{C}}}^{{\overline{\mathcal{M}_{\mathscr{C}}}},\overline{\sigma}}(\operatorname{\hbox to8.7pt{\vbox to8.7pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 0.25833pt\lower-1.76527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@closescope \hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {

and hence

.

(
7.6
)

y^{\prime}_{s,\alpha}\geq 0
holds by definition for all
s\in S_{\mathscr{C}}
,
\alpha\in\overline{\textit{Act}_{\mathscr{C}}}(s)
.

Thus, we have shown that the direction “
\Rightarrow
” holds.

\proofsubparagraph

“
\Leftarrow
”:
Let
y^{*}
be a feasible solution of
\Cref
fig:LP.
Let
S^{\textit{nn}}:=\{s\in S_{\mathscr{C}}\mid\sum_{\alpha\in\overline{\textit{Act}_{\mathscr{C}}}(s)}y^{*}_{s,\alpha}>0\}
.

We construct
\overline{\sigma}\in\Sigma^{\overline{\mathcal{M}_{\mathscr{C}}}}_{\textit{MR}}
as follows.
For
s\in\overline{S_{\mathscr{C}}}
,
\alpha\in\overline{\textit{Act}_{\mathscr{C}}}(s)
, let

\overline{\sigma}(s,\alpha)=\begin{cases}\frac{y^{*}_{s,\alpha}}{\sum_{\alpha^{\prime}\in\overline{\textit{Act}_{\mathscr{C}}}}y^{*}_{s^{\prime},\alpha}}&\text{if }s\in S^{\textit{nn}}\\
\frac{1}{|\overline{\textit{Act}_{\mathscr{C}}(s)}|}&\text{otherwise}~.\end{cases}

We define a vector
y^{\prime}
from
\overline{\sigma}
as in “
\Rightarrow
”, i.e., for
s\in S_{\mathscr{C}}
and
\alpha\in\overline{\textit{Act}_{\mathscr{C}}}(s)
, we let

\displaystyle y^{\prime}_{s,\alpha}

\displaystyle=\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}((\mathbf{P}_{\mathscr{C}}^{\overline{\sigma}})^{n})_{s_{\mathscr{C}},s}\cdot\overline{\sigma}(s,\alpha)

\displaystyle y^{\prime}_{s}

\displaystyle=\sum_{s^{\prime}\in S_{\mathscr{C}}}\sum_{\alpha^{\prime}\in\textit{Act}_{\mathscr{C}}(s^{\prime})}\mathbf{P}_{\mathscr{C}}(s^{\prime},\alpha^{\prime},s)\cdot y^{\prime}_{s^{\prime},\alpha^{\prime}}~.

Note that we do not know yet that these are well-defined.

In the following, we show that
S^{\textit{nn}}
contains all states reachable from
s_{\mathscr{C}}
under
\overline{\sigma}
, which we then use to show that the constructed vector
y^{\prime}
equals the feasible solution
y^{*}
and is thus well-defined.
Finally, we show that the constructed scheduler
\overline{\sigma}
satisfies the desired properties.

Let us first define some useful notation.
For
s\in S_{\mathscr{C}}
, let

\displaystyle y^{*}_{s}

\displaystyle=\sum_{s^{\prime}\in S_{\mathscr{C}}}\sum_{\alpha^{\prime}\in\textit{Act}_{\mathscr{C}}(s^{\prime})}\mathbf{P}_{\mathscr{C}}(s^{\prime},\alpha^{\prime},s)\cdot y^{*}_{s^{\prime},\alpha^{\prime}}

\displaystyle z^{*}_{s}

\displaystyle=\sum_{\alpha\in\overline{\textit{Act}_{\mathscr{C}}}(s)}y^{*}_{s,\alpha}~.

We define the set of all states that can ‘reach’
S_{\bot}
w.r.t.
y^{*}
as
W=

\left\{s\in S_{\mathscr{C}}\;\middle|\;\exists\pi=s_{0}\alpha_{0}\ldots s_{|\pi|}\in\textit{Paths}_{\textit{fin}}^{\overline{\mathcal{M}_{\mathscr{C}}}}(s).\ \prod_{i=0}^{|\pi|-1}y^{*}_{s_{i},\alpha_{i}}\cdot\overline{\mathbf{P}_{\mathscr{C}}}(s_{i},\alpha_{i},s_{i+1})>0\wedge s_{|\pi|}\in S_{\bot}\right\}~.

Observe that
S_{\bot}\cap W=\emptyset
by definition.
Observe further that
W\subseteq S^{\textit{nn}}
: By contraposition, let
s\not\in S^{\textit{nn}}
, then
y^{*}_{s,\alpha}=0
for all
\alpha\in\overline{\textit{Act}_{\mathscr{C}}}(s)
and hence there cannot exist any path satisfying the requirement for membership in
W
.

\proofsubparagraph

Claim:
S^{\textit{nn}}
contains all reachable states.

We show that for all
s\in\overline{S_{\mathscr{C}}}
reachable from
s_{\mathscr{C}}
under
\overline{\sigma}
, it must hold that
s\in S^{\textit{nn}}
, by induction on the length of the shortest path from
s_{\mathscr{C}}
to
s
.

IB:

s=s_{\mathscr{C}}
.
Since
y^{*}
is feasible, by constraint (
7.3
) we have

\sum_{\alpha\in\overline{\textit{Act}_{\mathscr{C}}}(s_{\mathscr{C}})}y^{*}_{s_{\mathscr{C}},\alpha}=\sum_{s^{\prime}\in S_{\mathscr{C}}}\sum_{\alpha^{\prime}\in\textit{Act}_{\mathscr{C}}(s^{\prime})}\mathbf{P}_{\mathscr{C}}(s^{\prime},\alpha^{\prime},s_{\mathscr{C}})\cdot y^{*}_{s^{\prime},\alpha^{\prime}}+1~.

Since
y^{*}_{s,\alpha}\geq 0
by constraint (
7.6
) for all
s\in S_{\mathscr{C}}
,
\alpha\in\overline{\textit{Act}_{\mathscr{C}}}(s)
, and
\mathbf{P}_{\mathscr{C}}(s^{\prime},\alpha^{\prime},s_{\mathscr{C}})\geq 0
by definition, we must have
\sum_{\alpha\in\overline{\textit{Act}_{\mathscr{C}}}(s_{\mathscr{C}})}y_{s_{\mathscr{C}},\alpha}>0
, i.e.,
s_{\mathscr{C}}\in S^{\textit{nn}}
.

IS:
Assume we can reach
s\in\overline{S_{\mathscr{C}}}
under
\overline{\sigma}
in
k>0
steps from
s_{\mathscr{C}}
with positive probability, and we cannot reach
s
in less than
k
steps.
There must exist some predecessor
s^{p}
of
s
that can be reached from
s_{\mathscr{C}}
in
k-1
steps.
Since
y^{*}
is feasible and thus satisfies constraint (
7.3
), we have

\displaystyle\sum_{\alpha\in\overline{\textit{Act}_{\mathscr{C}}}(s)}y^{*}_{s,\alpha}=\sum_{s^{\prime}\in S_{\mathscr{C}}}\sum_{\alpha^{\prime}\in\overline{\textit{Act}_{\mathscr{C}}}(s^{\prime})}y^{*}_{s^{\prime},\alpha^{\prime}}\overline{\mathbf{P}_{\mathscr{C}}}(s^{\prime},\alpha^{\prime},s)\geq\sum_{\alpha^{\prime}\in\overline{\textit{Act}_{\mathscr{C}}}(s^{p})}y^{*}_{s^{p},\alpha^{\prime}}\overline{\mathbf{P}_{\mathscr{C}}}(s^{p},\alpha^{\prime},s)\overset{\textit{IH}}{>}0~.

\proofsubparagraph

Claim:
y^{*}=y^{\prime}
on
W
.

We show that
y^{*}_{s,\alpha}=y^{\prime}_{s,\alpha}
for
s\in W\subseteq S^{\textit{nn}}
and
\alpha\in\overline{\textit{Act}_{\mathscr{C}}}(s)
, step by step.

•

We first show that
[s=s_{\mathscr{C}}]=z^{*}_{s}-\sum_{s^{\prime}\in S^{\textit{nn}}}(\mathbf{P}_{\mathscr{C}}^{\overline{\sigma}})_{s^{\prime},s}\cdot z^{*}_{s^{\prime}}
for all
s\in S^{\textit{nn}}
.
Since
y^{*}
satisfies constraint (
7.3
), we have

\displaystyle[s=s_{\mathscr{C}}]

\displaystyle=z^{*}_{s}-\sum_{s^{\prime}\in S_{\mathscr{C}}}\sum_{\alpha^{\prime}\in\overline{\textit{Act}_{\mathscr{C}}}}y^{*}_{s^{\prime},\alpha^{\prime}}\mathbf{P}_{\mathscr{C}}(s^{\prime},\alpha^{\prime},s)

\displaystyle=z^{*}_{s}-\sum_{s^{\prime}\in S^{\textit{nn}}}\sum_{\alpha^{\prime}\in\overline{\textit{Act}_{\mathscr{C}}}}y^{*}_{s^{\prime},\alpha^{\prime}}\mathbf{P}_{\mathscr{C}}(s^{\prime},\alpha^{\prime},s)\cdot\frac{\sum_{\alpha\in\overline{\textit{Act}_{\mathscr{C}}}(s)}y^{*}_{s,\alpha}}{\sum_{\alpha\in\overline{\textit{Act}_{\mathscr{C}}}(s)}y^{*}_{s,\alpha}}

\displaystyle=z^{*}_{s}-\sum_{s^{\prime}\in S^{\textit{nn}}}\sum_{\alpha^{\prime}\in\overline{\textit{Act}_{\mathscr{C}}}}\mathbf{P}_{\mathscr{C}}(s^{\prime},\alpha^{\prime},s)\cdot\overline{\sigma}(s^{\prime},\alpha^{\prime})\cdot z^{*}_{s^{\prime}}

\displaystyle=z^{*}_{s}-\sum_{s^{\prime}\in S^{\textit{nn}}}(\mathbf{P}_{\mathscr{C}}^{\overline{\sigma}})_{s^{\prime},s}\cdot z^{*}_{s^{\prime}}~.

•

Let us now show that for
s\in W
it holds that
\sum_{\alpha\in\overline{\textit{Act}_{\mathscr{C}}}}y^{*}_{s,\alpha}=\sum_{\alpha\in\overline{\textit{Act}_{\mathscr{C}}}}y^{\prime}_{s,\alpha}
.

Observe that
s\in W
implies that all predecessors of
s
must also be in
W
, and hence, using the above, for
s\in W
we have

\displaystyle[s=s_{\mathscr{C}}]=z^{*}_{s}-\sum_{s^{\prime}\in S^{\textit{nn}}}(\mathbf{P}_{\mathscr{C}}^{\overline{\sigma}})_{s^{\prime},s}\cdot z^{*}_{s^{\prime}}=z^{*}_{s}-\sum_{s^{\prime}\in W}(\mathbf{P}_{\mathscr{C}}^{\overline{\sigma}})_{s^{\prime},s}\cdot z^{*}_{s^{\prime}}~.

Let
\textbf{e}_{s_{\mathscr{C}}}
denote the unit vector for the index corresponding to
s_{\mathscr{C}}
(according to the order we fixed for constructing
\mathbf{P}_{\mathscr{C}}^{\overline{\sigma}}
).
Let
(\mathbf{P}_{\mathscr{C}}^{\overline{\sigma}})_{W,W}
denote the restriction of
\mathbf{P}_{\mathscr{C}}^{\overline{\sigma}}
to
W
.
Observe that
(\mathbf{P}_{\mathscr{C}}^{\overline{\sigma}})_{W,W}
must be sub-stochastic since all
s\in W
are transient, and hence
(I-(\mathbf{P}_{\mathscr{C}}^{\overline{\sigma}})_{W,W})^{-1}=\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}((\mathbf{P}_{\mathscr{C}}^{\overline{\sigma}})_{W,W})^{n}
exists.
Hence, we can rewrite
[s=s_{\mathscr{C}}]=z^{*}_{s}-\sum_{s^{\prime}\in W}(\mathbf{P}_{\mathscr{C}}^{\overline{\sigma}})_{s^{\prime},s}\cdot z^{*}_{s^{\prime}}
as

\textbf{e}_{s_{\mathscr{C}}}|_{W}=(z^{*})^{T}|_{W}\cdot(I-(\mathbf{P}_{\mathscr{C}}^{\overline{\sigma}})_{W,W})

and further as

(z^{*})^{T}|_{W}=\textbf{e}_{s_{\mathscr{C}}}|_{W}\cdot\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}((\mathbf{P}_{\mathscr{C}}^{\overline{\sigma}})_{W,W})^{n}~,

i.e.,
z^{*}_{s}=\sum_{i=0}^{\infty}((\mathbf{P}_{\mathscr{C}}^{\overline{\sigma}})^{i})_{s_{\mathscr{C}},s}
.
Thus, for
s\in W
we have

\displaystyle\sum_{\alpha\in\overline{\textit{Act}_{\mathscr{C}}}(s)}y^{*}_{s,\alpha}\overset{\textit{def.}}{=}z^{*}_{s}

\displaystyle=\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}((\mathbf{P}_{\mathscr{C}}^{\overline{\sigma}})^{n})_{s_{\mathscr{C}},s}=\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}((\mathbf{P}_{\mathscr{C}}^{\overline{\sigma}})^{n})_{s_{\mathscr{C}},s}\cdot\overbrace{\sum_{\alpha\in\overline{\textit{Act}_{\mathscr{C}}}(s)}\overline{\sigma}(s,\alpha)}^{=1}

\displaystyle=\sum_{\alpha\in\overline{\textit{Act}_{\mathscr{C}}}(s)}\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}((\mathbf{P}_{\mathscr{C}}^{\overline{\sigma}})^{n})_{s_{\mathscr{C}},s}\cdot\overline{\sigma}(s,\alpha)\overset{\textit{def.}}{=}\sum_{\alpha\in\overline{\textit{Act}_{\mathscr{C}}}(s)}y^{\prime}_{s,\alpha}~.

•

Finally, for
s\in W\subseteq S^{\textit{nn}}
and
\alpha\in\overline{\textit{Act}_{\mathscr{C}}}(s)
we have

\displaystyle y^{\prime}_{s,\alpha}

\displaystyle=\left(\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}((\mathbf{P}_{\mathscr{C}}^{\overline{\sigma}})^{n})_{s_{\mathscr{C}},s}\right)\cdot\frac{y^{*}_{s,\alpha}}{\sum_{\beta\in\overline{\textit{Act}_{\mathscr{C}}}}y^{*}_{s,\beta}}

// def. of
y^{\prime}
and
\overline{\sigma}

\displaystyle=\left(\sum_{\beta\in\overline{\textit{Act}_{\mathscr{C}}}(s)}y^{\prime}_{s,\beta}\right)\cdot\frac{y^{*}_{s,\alpha}}{\sum_{\beta\in\overline{\textit{Act}_{\mathscr{C}}}}y^{*}_{s,\beta}}

// analogously to “
\Rightarrow
”

\displaystyle=\left(\sum_{\beta\in\overline{\textit{Act}_{\mathscr{C}}}(s)}y^{\prime}_{s,\beta}\right)\cdot\frac{y^{*}_{s,\alpha}}{\sum_{\beta\in\overline{\textit{Act}_{\mathscr{C}}}}y^{\prime}_{s,\beta}}=y^{*}_{s,\alpha}

// by the above

\proofsubparagraph

Claim:
\overline{\sigma}
satisfies the required properties.

Analogously to “
\Rightarrow
” we can show that for the vector
y^{\prime}
we constructed from
\overline{\sigma}
, it holds that
\sum_{s\in S_{\mathscr{C}}}y^{\prime}_{s,\dagger}
is the expected number of times we move from
S_{\mathscr{C}}
to
S_{\bot}

for the first time
under
\overline{\sigma}
, i.e.,
{\text{Pr}}_{s_{\mathscr{C}}}^{{\overline{\mathcal{M}_{\mathscr{C}}}},\overline{\sigma}}(\operatorname{\hbox to8.7pt{\vbox to8.7pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 0.25833pt\lower-1.76527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@closescope \hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lxSVG@roundjoin\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-linejoin} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 3.57 L 5.66 9.23 L 11.32 3.57 L 5.66 -2.09 Z}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope }
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}S_{\bot})
, so

\displaystyle{\text{Pr}}_{s_{\mathscr{C}}}^{{\overline{\mathcal{M}_{\mathscr{C}}}},\overline{\sigma}}(\operatorname{\hbox to8.7pt{\vbox to8.7pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 0.25833pt\lower-1.76527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@closescope \hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lxSVG@roundjoin\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-linejoin} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 3.57 L 5.66 9.23 L 11.32 3.57 L 5.66 -2.09 Z}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope }
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}S_{\bot})=\sum_{s\in S_{\mathscr{C}}}y^{\prime}_{s,\dagger}

\displaystyle\geq\sum_{s\in W}y^{\prime}_{s,\dagger}

\displaystyle=\sum_{s\in W}y^{*}_{s,\dagger}

// by the above

\displaystyle=\sum_{s\in S_{\mathscr{C}}}y^{*}_{s,\dagger}

\displaystyle\text{// def.\ of }W

\displaystyle=1

//
y^{*}
is feasible sol.

This also implies that
y^{\prime}_{s}=0
for
s\in S_{\mathscr{C}}\setminus W
, since we would not reach
S_{\bot}
with probability 1 if we visited a state that cannot reach
S_{\bot}
.

Let
j=1,\ldots,l
.
Analogously to “
\Rightarrow
” we can show that

\mathbb{E}_{s_{\mathscr{C}}}^{{\overline{\mathcal{M}_{\mathscr{C}}}},\overline{\sigma}}(\textit{R}^{j})=\sum_{s\in S_{\mathscr{C}}}\textit{R}^{j}(s)y^{\prime}_{s}~.

From
y^{\prime}_{s}=0
for
s\in S_{\mathscr{C}}\setminus W
it follows further that

\sum_{s\in S_{\mathscr{C}}}\textit{R}^{j}(s)y^{\prime}_{s}=\sum_{s\in W}\textit{R}^{j}(s)y^{\prime}_{s}~.

Since
y^{\prime}_{s,\alpha}=y^{*}_{s,\alpha}
for
s\in W
,
\alpha\in\overline{\textit{Act}_{\mathscr{C}}}(s)
, and all predecessors of
s\in W
must also be in
W
, it follows that

y^{\prime}_{s}=\sum_{s^{\prime}\in W}\sum_{\alpha^{\prime}\in\textit{Act}_{\mathscr{C}}(s^{\prime})}\mathbf{P}_{\mathscr{C}}(s^{\prime},\alpha^{\prime},s)\cdot y^{\prime}_{s^{\prime},\alpha^{\prime}}=\sum_{s^{\prime}\in W}\sum_{\alpha^{\prime}\in\textit{Act}_{\mathscr{C}}(s^{\prime})}\mathbf{P}_{\mathscr{C}}(s^{\prime},\alpha^{\prime},s)\cdot y^{*}_{s^{\prime},\alpha^{\prime}}=y^{*}_{s}

for
s\in W
and thus

\sum_{s\in W}\textit{R}^{j}(s)y^{\prime}_{s}=\sum_{s\in W}\textit{R}^{j}(s)y^{*}_{s}~.

By assumption,
\sum_{s\in S_{\mathscr{C}}}\textit{R}^{j}(s)y^{*}_{s}=x_{j}
and hence
\mathbb{E}_{s_{\mathscr{C}}}^{{\overline{\mathcal{M}_{\mathscr{C}}}},\overline{\sigma}}(\textit{R}^{j})=x_{j}
as required.
∎

G.3
Proof of
\Cref
th:MOA_correctness

See
82

Proof.

Correctness follows from
\Cref
le:conj_LP and
\Cref
th:moa_preproc_transfer-MR.

For the runtime complexity:
Observe that the linear real arithmetic encoding is not necessarily a linear program, whose standard form only allows
\leq,\geq
-constraints, due to the presence of
\approx_{\epsilon}
-,
>
-, and
\not\approx_{\epsilon}
-predicates in the encoding.
However, we can solve the constructed linear real arithmetic encoding (
\Cref
fig:LP) using successive calls to LP solvers, as outlined in
\Cref
alg:LP. We show that this approach is correct and yields the desired runtime complexity in
\Cref
th:lra_via_lp.
∎

Input:

Linear real arithmetic encoding
L
from
\Cref
fig:LP with comparison operators
\{>,\geq,\approx_{\epsilon},\not\approx_{\epsilon}\mid\epsilon\in\mathbb{Q}_{\geq 0}\}

Output:

Does
L
have a feasible optimal solution?

L^{\prime}\leftarrow

EliminateStrict
(
EliminateEquality
(
L
))

//
See p.
G.3

D^{\prime}\leftarrow

\not\approx_{\epsilon}
-constraints of
L^{\prime}
with
\epsilon>0

1

for

J\subseteq D^{\prime}

do

//
Check all possibilities for satisfying all
\not\approx
-constraints

2

J^{<}\leftarrow

EliminateStrict
(
J
with
x\not\approx_{\epsilon}y
replaced by
x<y+\epsilon
)

3

(D^{\prime}\setminus J)^{>}\leftarrow

EliminateStrict
(
D^{\prime}\setminus J
with
x\not\approx_{\epsilon}y
repl. by
x>y-\epsilon
)

4

L^{\prime\prime}\leftarrow

L^{\prime}
with
J
replaced by
J^{<}\wedge(D^{\prime}\setminus J)^{>}

5

C\leftarrow

\leq,\geq
-constraints of
L^{\prime\prime}

6

d_{1},\ldots,d_{k}\leftarrow

\neq
-constraints of
L^{\prime\prime}

7

for

i=1,\ldots,k

do

//
Check
\neq
-constraints one by one

8

d_{i}^{<}\leftarrow

EliminateStrict
(
d_{i}
with
\neq
replaced by

)

9

d_{i}^{>}\leftarrow

EliminateStrict
(
d_{i}
with

)

,

,

does not have feasible opt. sol.

then
