Algorithm 8

Solving linear real arithmetic for MOA queries with comparison operators
\{>,\geq,\approx_{\epsilon},\not\approx_{\epsilon}\mid\epsilon\in\mathbb{Q}_{\geq 0}\}
via LPs

Theorem 96
.

\Cref

alg:LP adheres to its input-output specification and can be implemented with worst-case running time of
\mathcal{O}(2^{l_{\not\approx}}\cdot\textit{poly}(l\cdot m\cdot|\mathcal{M}|\cdot 2^{l\cdot m}))

where
l
is the number of predicates,
l_{\not\approx}
the number of predicates using
\not\approx_{\epsilon}
with
\epsilon>0
, and
m
the number of probability operators per conjunct in the original
MORelReach
property.

Proof.

Correctness:

•

\boldsymbol{\approx_{\epsilon}}
:
We can eliminate
\approx_{\epsilon}
-constraints by transforming them to a conjunction of a
\leq
-constraint and a
\geq
-constraint (
x\approx_{\epsilon}y\Leftrightarrow x\geq y-\epsilon\land x\leq y+\epsilon
).

•

\boldsymbol{<,>}
:
We can eliminate
<,>
-constraints by introducing a new variable
z
, replacing the constraint
x<y
(or
x>y
) with
x\leq y-z
(or
x\geq y+z
) and additionally adding the goal of maximizing
z
(see, e.g.,
[
21
,
44
]
).
If there exists an optimal solution to the resulting system of inequalities that assigns a positive value to
z
, then there must exist a feasible solution to the original problem, and vice versa.

Repeatedly eliminating strict constraints yields multiple optimization objectives, i.e., we end up with a multi-objective LP.

•

\boldsymbol{\neq}
:
Assume a linear real arithmetic encoding
L
containing only
\neq,\leq,\geq
-constraints.
Let
F
be the set of solutions for the encoding without the
\neq
-constraints, then
F
must be convex since the encoding without the
\neq
-constraints forms an LP.
Observe that each
\neq
-constraint only excludes a hyperplane from
F
.
Assume the whole equation system
L
cannot be satisfied but
F
is non-empty, then
F
must be contained in the union of the hyperplanes corresponding to the
\neq
-constraints.
Assume
F
is contained in more than a single such hyperplane, then the solution space would not be convex, which is a contradiction.
Hence,
F
must be contained in a single hyperplane corresponding to one of the
\neq
-equations.

Thus, we can solve
L
by first solving the equation system without the
\neq
-constraints and then checking the
\neq
-constraints one by one, see also
[
41
, Th. 1]
.
In other words, we can solve a linear real arithmetic encoding containing only
\neq,\leq,\geq
-constraints by solving a
linear
number of LPs.

•

\boldsymbol{\not\approx_{\epsilon}}
:
For
\not\approx_{\epsilon}
-constraints with
\epsilon>0
the trick from
\neq
does not work since now a single
\not\approx_{\epsilon}
-constraint may exclude more than just a hyperplane and the union of the areas covered by the
\not\approx_{\epsilon}
-constraints may be convex.

Instead, each
\not\approx_{\epsilon}
-constraint introduces a disjunction (
x\not\approx_{\epsilon}y\Leftrightarrow x<y-\epsilon\lor x>y+\epsilon
).
Let
L
be the original encoding, let
L^{<}
be
L
with the
\not\approx_{\epsilon}
-constraint replaced by the
<
-disjunct, and let
L^{>}
be
L
with the
\not\approx_{\epsilon}
-constraint replaced by the
>
-disjunct.
Then
L
has a solution if either
L^{<}
or
L^{>}
have a solution.

Hence, we can solve a linear real arithmetic encoding
L
containing only
\not\approx_{\epsilon},\leq,\geq
-constraints by solving an
exponential
number of LPs.

Runtime:
If the query contains
l_{\not\approx}
constraint with
\not\approx_{\epsilon}
with
\epsilon>0
, then
\Cref
alg:LP solves up to
2^{l_{\not\approx}}
queries with only
=
,
\neq
and
\leq
constraints.
If the query does not contain
\not\approx_{\epsilon}
with
\epsilon>0
:
Let
l_{\neq}
be the number of
\neq
-constraints in the linear real arithmetic encoding, then
\Cref
alg:LP solves up to
l_{\neq}
LPs with
=
- and
\leq
-constraints.
For each LP, the number of constraints is
|S_{X}|+|X|+|S_{X}|+|S_{X}|\cdot|\overline{\textit{Act}_{X}}|\in\mathcal{O}(\textit{poly}(|S_{X}|\cdot|\overline{\textit{Act}_{X}}|))
.
Observe

|S_{X}|\cdot|\overline{\textit{Act}_{X}}|\leq(l\cdot m\cdot|S|\cdot 2^{l\cdot m}+1)\cdot(|\textit{Act}|+2)
, hence
each constructed LP can be solved in time
\mathcal{O}(\textit{poly}(l\cdot m\cdot|S|\cdot 2^{l\cdot m}\cdot|\textit{Act}|))
since LPs can be solved in time polynomial in the number of constraints
[
37
]
.
∎

Appendix H
Proofs for
\Cref
sec:conj_complexity

H.1
Proof of
\Cref
th:conj_special

See
84

We have already argued for membership in
PTIME
and
EXPTIME
in
\Cref
sec:conj_complexity.
It remains to show that
MORelReach
is strongly
NP
-hard in cases
\mathit{(a)}
and
\mathit{(b)}
.
We do so by showing the following lemma:

Lemma 97
.

MORelReach
is strongly
NP
-hard even if
T_{1,1}=\ldots=T_{m,l}
and this target set is absorbing.

Proof.

We show strong
NP
-hardness by reduction from 3SAT
[
36
]
.
Given a 3SAT formula
\varphi=\bigwedge_{j=1}^{M}(l_{j,1}\lor l_{j,2}\lor l_{j,3})
over variables
x_{1},\ldots,x_{N}
with literals
l_{j,h}\in\{x_{i},\overline{x_{i}}\mid i\in\{1,\ldots,N\}\}
for
j\in\{1,\ldots,M\}
,
h\in\{1,2,3\}
.
We construct the MDP
\mathcal{M}_{\varphi}=(s_{\varphi},\textit{Act}_{\varphi},\mathbf{P}_{\varphi})
, depicted in
\Cref
fig:conj_NP-hard, with

•

s_{\varphi}=\{s,t\}
,

•

\textit{Act}_{\varphi}=\{\alpha,\beta\}
, and

•

\mathbf{P}_{\varphi}(s,\alpha,s)=1
,
\mathbf{P}_{\varphi}(s,\beta,t)=1
,
\mathbf{P}_{\varphi}(t,\beta,t)=1
, and all other transition probabilities are 0.

We construct the following
MORelReach
property with a single, absorbing target set
T=\{t\}
:

\displaystyle\varphi^{\prime}=\exists\sigma_{1},\ldots,\sigma_{N}\in\Sigma^{\mathcal{M}_{\varphi}}.\

\displaystyle\bigwedge_{j=1}^{N}{\text{Pr}}^{\sigma_{i}}_{s}(\operatorname{\hbox to8.7pt{\vbox to8.7pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 0.25833pt\lower-1.76527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@closescope \hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lxSVG@roundjoin\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-linejoin} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 3.57 L 5.66 9.23 L 11.32 3.57 L 5.66 -2.09 Z}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope }
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}T)\not\approx_{\nicefrac{{1}}{{4}}}\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}\land\bigwedge_{j=1}^{M}\sum_{h=1}^{3}\alpha_{j,1}\left({\text{Pr}}^{\sigma_{k_{j,1}}}_{s}(\operatorname{\hbox to8.7pt{\vbox to8.7pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 0.25833pt\lower-1.76527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@closescope \hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lxSVG@roundjoin\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-linejoin} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 3.57 L 5.66 9.23 L 11.32 3.57 L 5.66 -2.09 Z}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope }
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}T)-\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}\right)\geq-1

where

\alpha_{j,h}=\begin{cases}2&\text{if }l_{j,h}\text{ is positive}\\
-2&\text{otherwise}\end{cases}

and

k_{j,h}
is the unique
i\in\{1,\ldots,N\}
with
l_{j,h}\in\{x_{i},\overline{x_{i}}\}
.
We now show that
\varphi
is satisfiable iff
\varphi^{\prime}
holds.

‘
\Rightarrow
’:
Assume
\varphi
is satisfiable, let
\mathcal{I}\colon\{x_{1},\ldots,x_{N}\}\to\{0,1\}
be a satisfying interpretation.
For
i\in\{1,\ldots,N\}
, we construct a memoryless deterministic scheduler
\sigma_{i}\in\Sigma^{\mathcal{M}_{\varphi}}
by letting
\sigma(s)(\beta)=\mathcal{I}(x_{i})
and
\sigma(s)(\beta)=1-\mathcal{I}(x_{i})
.
Then,
{\text{Pr}}^{\sigma_{i}}_{s}(\operatorname{\hbox to8.7pt{\vbox to8.7pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 0.25833pt\lower-1.76527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@closescope \hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lxSVG@roundjoin\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-linejoin} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 3.57 L 5.66 9.23 L 11.32 3.57 L 5.66 -2.09 Z}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope }
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}T)=\mathcal{I}(x_{i})\not\approx_{\nicefrac{{1}}{{4}}}\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}
for
i\in\{1,\ldots,N\}
and hence the first part of
\varphi^{\prime}
holds.
Further, for
j\in\{1,\ldots,M\}
and
h\in\{1,2,3\}
, we have

\displaystyle\alpha_{j,h}\left({\text{Pr}}^{\sigma_{k_{j,h}}}_{s}(\operatorname{\hbox to8.7pt{\vbox to8.7pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 0.25833pt\lower-1.76527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@closescope \hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lxSVG@roundjoin\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-linejoin} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 3.57 L 5.66 9.23 L 11.32 3.57 L 5.66 -2.09 Z}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope }
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}T)-\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}\right)=\begin{cases}2\cdot\left(1-\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}\right)=1&\text{if }l_{j,h}=x_{k_{j,h}}\land\mathcal{I}(x_{k_{j,h}})=1\\
2\cdot\left(0-\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}\right)=-1&\text{if }l_{j,h}=x_{k_{j,h}}\land\mathcal{I}(x_{k_{j,h}})=0\\
-2\cdot\left(1-\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}\right)=-1&\text{if }l_{j,h}=\overline{x_{k_{j,h}}}\land\mathcal{I}(x_{k_{j,h}})=1\\
-2\cdot\left(0-\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}\right)=1&\text{if }l_{j,h}=\overline{x_{k_{j,h}}}\land\mathcal{I}(x_{k_{j,h}})=0\end{cases}

Since
\mathcal{I}
is a satisfying interpretation, for each
j\in\{1,\ldots,M\}
there must exist at least one
h\in\{1,3\}
with
l_{j,h}
being positive, and hence
\sum_{h=1}^{3}\alpha_{j,1}\left({\text{Pr}}^{\sigma_{k_{j,1}}}_{s}(\operatorname{\hbox to8.7pt{\vbox to8.7pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 0.25833pt\lower-1.76527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@closescope \hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lxSVG@roundjoin\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-linejoin} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 3.57 L 5.66 9.23 L 11.32 3.57 L 5.66 -2.09 Z}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope }
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}T)-\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}\right)\in\{-1,1,3\}
and thus the second part of
\varphi^{\prime}
holds.

‘
\Leftarrow
’:
Assume
\varphi^{\prime}
holds. Let
\sigma_{1},\ldots,\sigma_{N}
be witnesses for
\varphi^{\prime}
.
Then for each
i\in\{1,\ldots,N\}
it must hold that
{\text{Pr}}^{\sigma_{i}}_{s}(\operatorname{\hbox to8.7pt{\vbox to8.7pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 0.25833pt\lower-1.76527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@closescope \hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lxSVG@roundjoin\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-linejoin} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 3.57 L 5.66 9.23 L 11.32 3.57 L 5.66 -2.09 Z}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope }
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}T)\not\approx_{\nicefrac{{1}}{{4}}}\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}
, i.e.,
{\text{Pr}}^{\sigma_{i}}_{s}(\operatorname{\hbox to8.7pt{\vbox to8.7pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 0.25833pt\lower-1.76527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@closescope \hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lxSVG@roundjoin\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-linejoin} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 3.57 L 5.66 9.23 L 11.32 3.57 L 5.66 -2.09 Z}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope }
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}T)\in[0,\nicefrac{{1}}{{4}})\cup(\nicefrac{{3}}{{4}},1]
.
Hence, the following interpretation for
\varphi
is well-defined:

\mathcal{I}(x_{i})=\begin{cases}1&\text{if }{\text{Pr}}^{\sigma_{i}}_{s}(\operatorname{\hbox to8.7pt{\vbox to8.7pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 0.25833pt\lower-1.76527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@closescope \hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lxSVG@roundjoin\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-linejoin} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 3.57 L 5.66 9.23 L 11.32 3.57 L 5.66 -2.09 Z}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope }
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}T)>\nicefrac{{3}}{{4}}\\
0&\text{if }{\text{Pr}}^{\sigma_{i}}_{s}(\operatorname{\hbox to8.7pt{\vbox to8.7pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 0.25833pt\lower-1.76527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@closescope \hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lxSVG@roundjoin\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-linejoin} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 3.57 L 5.66 9.23 L 11.32 3.57 L 5.66 -2.09 Z}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope }
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}T)<\nicefrac{{1}}{{4}}\end{cases}

Let us now see that
\mathcal{I}
is a satisfying interpretation for
\varphi
.
From the above observation on
{\text{Pr}}^{\sigma_{i}}_{s}(\operatorname{\hbox to8.7pt{\vbox to8.7pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 0.25833pt\lower-1.76527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@closescope \hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lxSVG@roundjoin\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-linejoin} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 3.57 L 5.66 9.23 L 11.32 3.57 L 5.66 -2.09 Z}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope }
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}T)
it follows that for
j\in\{1,\ldots,M\}
and
h\in\{1,2,3\}
we have
{\text{Pr}}^{\sigma_{k_{j,1}}}_{s}(\operatorname{\hbox to8.7pt{\vbox to8.7pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 0.25833pt\lower-1.76527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@closescope \hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lxSVG@roundjoin\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-linejoin} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 3.57 L 5.66 9.23 L 11.32 3.57 L 5.66 -2.09 Z}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope }
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}T)-\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}\in[-\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}},-\nicefrac{{1}}{{4}})\cup(\nicefrac{{1}}{{4}},\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}]
and thus

\displaystyle\alpha_{j,h}\left({\text{Pr}}^{\sigma_{k_{j,h}}}_{s}(\operatorname{\hbox to8.7pt{\vbox to8.7pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 0.25833pt\lower-1.76527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@closescope \hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lxSVG@roundjoin\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-linejoin} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 3.57 L 5.66 9.23 L 11.32 3.57 L 5.66 -2.09 Z}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope }
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}T)-\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}\right)\in\begin{cases}(\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}},1]&\text{if }l_{j,h}=x_{k_{j,h}}\land\mathcal{I}(x_{k_{j,h}})=1\\
[-1,-\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}})&\text{if }l_{j,h}=x_{k_{j,h}}\land\mathcal{I}(x_{k_{j,h}})=0\\
[-1,-\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}})&\text{if }l_{j,h}=\overline{x_{k_{j,h}}}\land\mathcal{I}(x_{k_{j,h}})=1\\
(\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}},1]&\text{if }l_{j,h}=\overline{x_{k_{j,h}}}\land\mathcal{I}(x_{k_{j,h}})=0\end{cases}

By assumption, for
j\in\{1,\ldots,M\}
it must hold that
\sum_{h=1}^{3}\alpha_{j,h}\left({\text{Pr}}^{\sigma_{k_{j,h}}}_{s}(\operatorname{\hbox to8.7pt{\vbox to8.7pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 0.25833pt\lower-1.76527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@closescope \hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lxSVG@roundjoin\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-linejoin} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 3.57 L 5.66 9.23 L 11.32 3.57 L 5.66 -2.09 Z}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope }
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}T)-\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}\right)\geq-1
, and hence there must exists at least one
h\in\{1,2,3\}
with
\alpha_{j,h}\left({\text{Pr}}^{\sigma_{k_{j,h}}}_{s}(\operatorname{\hbox to8.7pt{\vbox to8.7pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 0.25833pt\lower-1.76527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@closescope \hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lxSVG@roundjoin\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-linejoin} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 3.57 L 5.66 9.23 L 11.32 3.57 L 5.66 -2.09 Z}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope }
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}T)-\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}\right)>\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}
, i.e., with
l_{j,h}=x_{k_{j,h}}\land\mathcal{I}(x_{k_{j,h}})=1
or
l_{j,h}=\overline{x_{k_{j,h}}}\land\mathcal{I}(x_{k_{j,h}})=0
.
Hence, under
\mathcal{I}
, for each clause
j\in\{1,\ldots,M\}
at least one literal holds and thus
\mathcal{I}
is a satisfying interpretation for
\varphi
.

It remains to show that the above construction defines a pseudo-polynomial time transformation to
MORelReach
.
The constructed MDP has a fixed number of states and transitions, and all transition probabilities are 0 or 1.
The property quantifies over
N
schedulers and contains a conjunction of size
N
as well as a conjunction of size
M
, where
N
is the number of variables and
M
the number of clauses in the original 3SAT formula. All coefficients and bounds in the property (
\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}
,
\nicefrac{{1}}{{4}}
,
-1
) are fixed.
Hence, the magnitude of the largest number occurring in the constructed
MORelReach
instance is polynomial in the size of the 3SAT instance.
∎

t

\beta

.

Appendix I
Details on the Benchmarks for Probabilistic Hyperproperties (
\Cref
sec:hyperprob-benchmarks)

The contents of this section are taken verbatim from
[
27
]
.

TA

A standard application of probabilistic hyperproperties
is to check whether an implementation of modular exponentiation for RSA public-key encryption
has a side-channel
timing leak

[
2
]
.
Concretely, the setting is as follows: One thread computes
a^{b}\text{ mod }n
for a given plaintext
a
, encryption key
b
, and modulus
n
(all non-negative integers),
while a second (attacker) thread tries to infer the value of
b
by keeping a counter
c
to measure the time taken by the first thread.

\Cref
fig:modexp shows the implementation of the modular exponentiation algorithm that we want to check for side-channels.

If
b
has
k
bits, we can model the parallel execution of the two threads as an MDP with
2^{k}
initial states
Init
, each representing a different value of
b
.
In our models, a (memoryful randomized) scheduler for the MDP corresponds to a thread scheduler.
In contrast, in
[
1
,
6
]
the authors hard-coded fair thread schedulers in their models, and a (memoryless deterministic) scheduler for the MDP corresponds to a secret input.

The goal is to now check whether the implementation satisfies
scheduler-specific probabilistic observational determinism
(SSPOD)
[
45
]
, i.e., whether for any scheduling of the two threads that no information about the secret input (here,
b
) can be inferred by the publicly observable information (here, the time taken by the modular exponentiation thread).

In
[
2
]
, the desired property is formulated in
HyperPCTL
as follows:

\forall\hat{\sigma}_{1}\forall\hat{\sigma}_{2}.\ \forall\hat{s}_{1}(\hat{\sigma}_{1})\forall\hat{s}_{2}(\hat{\sigma}_{2}).\ (\textit{init}_{\hat{s}_{1}}\wedge\textit{init}_{\hat{s}_{2}})\Rightarrow\bigwedge_{j=0}^{2k}\mathbb{P}(\operatorname{\hbox to8.7pt{\vbox to8.7pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 0.25833pt\lower-1.76527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@closescope \hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lxSVG@roundjoin\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-linejoin} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 3.57 L 5.66 9.23 L 11.32 3.57 L 5.66 -2.09 Z}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope }

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lxSVG@roundjoin\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-linejoin} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 3.57 L 5.66 9.23 L 11.32 3.57 L 5.66 -2.09 Z}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope }
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}(c=j)_{\hat{s}_{2}})

where

init
marks initial states for different values of the secret input
b
,

b
has
k

is the counter of the attacking side-channel thread.

:

TS

Consider the following classic example of a simple insecure multi-threaded program
[
54
]

th\colon\text{\bf{while }}h>0\text{\bf{ do }}\{h\leftarrow h-1\};\;l\leftarrow 2\quad\parallel\quad th^{\prime}\colon l\leftarrow 1

where
h
is a secret input and
l
a public output.
Intuitively, this program is not secure because the final value of
l
allows to make a probabilistic inference on the initial value of the secret input
h
.
For example, under a fair scheduler (that schedules all available threads with the same probability), the probability of outputting
l=2
is much higher than the probability of outputting
l=1
.
Formally, the program violates
scheduler-specific probabilistic observational determinism
(SSPOD)
[
45
]
.
SSPOD stipulates that for all schedulings of the threads
the probability of observing
l=1
in the end should be the same as the probability of observing
l=2
.

[
2
]
formulates the desired property in
HyperPCTL
as follows:

\forall\hat{\sigma}.\ \forall\hat{s}_{1}(\hat{\sigma})\forall\hat{s}_{2}(\hat{\sigma}).\ (\textit{init}_{\hat{s}_{1}}\wedge\textit{init}_{\hat{s}_{2}})\Rightarrow\bigwedge_{j=1}^{2}\mathbb{P}(\operatorname{\hbox to8.7pt{\vbox to8.7pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 0.25833pt\lower-1.76527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@closescope \hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lxSVG@roundjoin\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-linejoin} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 3.57 L 5.66 9.23 L 11.32 3.57 L 5.66 -2.09 Z}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope }
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}(l=j)_{\hat{s}_{1}})=\mathbb{P}(\operatorname{\hbox to8.7pt{\vbox to8.7pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 0.25833pt\lower-1.76527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@closescope \hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {

where

init
marks initial states for different values of the secret input
h
.

This is equivalent to the following conjunction of universally quantified relational reachability properties:

\displaystyle\bigwedge_{s_{1},s_{2}\in\textit{Init}}\bigwedge_{j=1}^{2}\forall\sigma.\ {\text{Pr}}^{\sigma}_{s_{1}}(\operatorname{\hbox to8.7pt{\vbox to8.7pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 0.25833pt\lower-1.76527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@closescope \hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {

SD

[
6
]
extend the notion of
stochastic domination

[
10
]
to MDP states by defining that a state
s_{1}
stochastically dominates a state
s_{2}
w.r.t. a target set
T
iff

\forall\sigma.\ {\text{Pr}}^{\sigma}_{s_{1}}(\operatorname{\hbox to8.7pt{\vbox to8.7pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 0.25833pt\lower-1.76527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@closescope \hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lxSVG@roundjoin\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-linejoin} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 3.57 L 5.66 9.23 L 11.32 3.57 L 5.66 -2.09 Z}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope }
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}T)\geq{\text{Pr}}^{\sigma}_{s_{2}}(\operatorname{\hbox to8.7pt{\vbox to8.7pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 0.25833pt\lower-1.76527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@closescope \hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lxSVG@roundjoin\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-linejoin} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 3.57 L 5.66 9.23 L 11.32 3.57 L 5.66 -2.09 Z}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope }
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}T)~,

which is a natural universally quantified relational reachability property.
In
[
6
]
, this property is applied to various robot-maze problems with a fixed pair of initial locations each, checking whether the first location can guarantee a better reachability probability than the other location, no matter how the robot behaves.
We check the same property (over memoryful randomized schedulers) on all mazes provided in
[
6
]
.
